The help pictures that show the button use the same class, so the picture and the button behave alike. Test: `tests/teacher-mark-attendance-call.test.ts`. First numbered D-535, which PR 306 took. |

## The Menu tab, redrawn for staff new to apps, with Settings first — 7 November 2026

Janmejay, 7 Nov 2026: "redesign the menu section in the mobile screen, the menu tab · optimize the setting tab to be accessible quickly · rework the labels and buttons in the menu app · brainstorm creative ideas and apply it so it is easy to understand and quickly usable. And deploy changes to live."

| Decision | Outcome | Evidence and reason |
|---|---|---|
| D-519 | **The Menu opens with a Settings card: the person (photo, greeting, role), five quick switches that say their state in words (Dark mode, Language, Sounds, Vibration, Read aloud), one big button to all settings (the school's for an admin, "My settings" for everyone else) and three small ones (My settings, Password, School year). Below it: "Your shortcuts" with Change, group chips that stay on top and jump down the page, then every screen as a big tile in a coloured group with one line saying what it opens. "See One as…" for the admin, and Sign out asks once. A Listen button reads out what is where and lights each part.** | Settings was the fifth tile of the last group, about 1,900 px down an admin's phone; a non-admin had no word "Settings" on the Menu at all (their own settings were "Language and start screen" at the bottom). The tiles were names only ("Substitutions", "Master data", "Activity log", "Money words"), which a teacher new to apps cannot place; each now has a plain line under it in the reader's language, and several were renamed ("Cover for absent teachers", "Classes, routes & discounts", "Who changed what", "Bus students"). Online payments, in the desk sidebar but missing from the phone, is a tile now; Settings is no longer a tile because the card is above everything. A non-admin's default shortcuts were their four bottom tabs, so they now skip what the bar already opens. The quick switches lay out three to a row with no gap (two by two where a desk or an iPhone has four). Signing out by accident left staff who do not remember their password locked out, so it asks first. Haptics through `cue()` (select on a switch or chip, toast cue on save), the reading highlight reuses `.vx-step.reading` (D-497), and the tiles rise in with a short stagger that reduced motion turns off. Code: `src/app/(app)/menu/*` (styles in `menu.css`), `src/vx/shell/menu-prefs.tsx`; tests `tests/vx-menu-items.test.ts`; fixture views `F13-Menu`, `F13-Menu-Accountant`. |

## The small things: faces, a year switch you can see, taps that answer — 7 November 2026

Janmejay, 7 Nov 2026, after writing a note on the real year: "the student image didn't show … when I was switching the session pills, there was no animation, like these are the small small things that matter … go through the one app thoroughly … find those bugs and fixes and improvements … for non-technical teachers … and deploy all the changes to live". On teachers' photos he chose (14:06 UTC) that One keeps its own copy, made from Fees, and approved the One database change (14:19 UTC). Findings and plan: Claude Doc "One polish for teachers: findings and fixes".

| Decision | Outcome | Evidence and reason |
|---|---|---|
| D-511 | **Teachers see the real year's photos from One's own copy of the Fees photos, made by an admin's "Refresh roster now"; nothing is written to Fees** | The photos live in Fees, which shares them through One only with office staff, so a teacher saw initials for every real child. The same tap that refreshes the roster now copies, in batches with a bar and a count, each photo One does not hold yet: children on the roll only, through Fees' photo pages (40 a page) as that admin, made into One's standard photo and placed by `place_student_photo` with the new source `fees`. A photo a person placed in One is never replaced from Fees; a copy made from an older Fees photo is replaced, through the optimistic check. The command id is derived from child, Fees photo and the photo it replaces, so a retry after a lost answer replays instead of copying twice. Route `POST /api/fees-bridge/photos/copy`: same origin, a real admin, not previewing; a TEST year copies nothing. Migration `20261107142035` allows the source (owner's go-ahead, 14:19 UTC). Not automatic: Janmejay chose the tap, and a nightly copy would need a machine actor that One does not have. |
| D-512 | **"Current in Fees" follows the nightly roster: the identity views accept a roster checked within 26 hours, not 5 minutes** | Since D-457 the roster is copied once a night, but `fees_identity_students` and `fees_identity_classes` still demanded a check within five minutes, so between the nightly copy and an admin's refresh every real-year save that asks "is this child current?" (attendance, marks, tasks, notices, a placed photo) was refused. 26 hours is `BRIDGE_FRESH_FOR_MS`, the window the rest of One already uses; the views are rebuilt from their own definitions with only the interval changed and stay `security_invoker`. The publication guard keeps its five minutes: it only guards the switch into publication. Same migration. |
| D-513 | **Wherever a note names a child, the child's face is there: New note's class list, every card's header, the check card's "move it to" rows, the Saved card, the board, the teacher's cards, a note's page and the child's "All notes"** | `childChoices()` read each child's approved photo and dropped it, and `BoardNote` had no photo at all, so every note screen drew initials even where the photo existed. One `Face` (`src/vx/ui/face.tsx`) puts the photo over the initials: the initials show until it loads, it fades in, and a photo that fails (an expired signed link, a phone gone offline) leaves the initials rather than a broken image. Each screen keeps its own circle, colour and size. With a photo in the circle, the roll number moves under the name. |
| D-514 | **Every link answers the tap at once: it dims while its page is on the way, the top bar is thicker, a tab lights the moment it is tapped, and a page slower than half a second says "Opening…" above the tabs** | A phone tap on a slow page looked like nothing had happened for up to three seconds, and the two-pixel bar was missed. `@/ui/link` now reports its own pending state (`LinkPendingReporter`, Next's `useLinkStatus`), as `NavLink` did, so screen links, tabs and the side menu all share one signal. "Opening…" belongs to the address it was shown on and goes the moment the new page arrives; a year switch has its own card, so the note stays out of its way. The tapped tab pulses gently until its page arrives and lets go after eight seconds if it never does. |
| D-515 | **Changing the year is seen: the screen dims, a card says "Opening 2026-27…" over a calendar turning its page, then "Now showing 2026-27" with a tick for under two seconds; test years are labelled "Practice years (test data only)"** | The phone pill cleared the switching flag the moment the cookie was written, before the new year had loaded, so nothing showed. The flag now stays on until the refresh ends. The pill names the year it is opening and clears the name on a failure, so a failure never reads "Now showing". A short tap cue at the start and a success cue at the end; reduced motion gets the cards without movement. "Test / UAT / DEMO" told a teacher nothing. |
| D-516 | **Smaller fixes from the smoke: Messages tabs wrap into whole buttons on a phone and the "+" says "New"; Calendar's "+" says "Add" and the month comes before the board's deadlines; every Settings row ends in the same ›; the notes board's four counts are the tabs on a phone, so the first note is on the first screen** | On a 360-pixel phone the five Messages tabs ran together ("Replies (2) Formats") and Staff was cut off; the not-sent heading repeated what each row says. A bare "+" told a new teacher nothing. The exam office card filled Calendar's first screen. Settings mixed → and › in one list. The board's tiles and its tab strip were the same four choices twice, and pushed the notes below the fold. |
| D-517 | **The year picker says every word through the fee catalogue, on the desk and the phone alike, and a reader of both languages reads it in both: "Practice years · अभ्यास वर्ष"** | The live check after D-515 found the desktop menu still headed the test years "Test / UAT / DEMO" (and the archived ones "Other production"): it carried English titles of its own, while the phone sheet said "Practice years". `groupSessions` now names each group by its catalogue key and both pills render the key, as do the desk menu's "Changing to…", loading line, TEST badge, trigger label and failure toast. The phone sheet showed an admin who reads both languages Hindi alone because the root layout cut the shell catalogue from a fresh import of `hi.json`; it now cuts it from the catalogue `i18n/request.ts` already built for the request (`getMessages()`), which for that reader is the bilingual one the fee screens use (A-23), so the year switch card is in both too. The heading is shortened to "Practice years" so the pair fits one line; each row's TEST badge says it is test data. The One pill also stopped tightening a long label's letters, which made it 3 px narrower than its loading placeholder, so it jumped as the year list arrived. |
| D-518 | **The shell's words follow the account's language straight after sign-in, and after the EN \| हि switch, with no reload** | BrowserStack (Galaxy M32 and Windows, after D-517) found the year picker in English right after sign-in for an admin who reads both languages, and in both after a reload. The root layout's fee `LanguageProvider` was handed the account's locale and catalogue again (the sign-in server action's redirect and `router.refresh()` both re-render the root layout; a probe of the live provider showed `initialLocale` "hi" beside a state of "en"), but it had copied its first props into state and never looked again. It now takes the server's locale and catalogue whenever `catalogs` arrives as a new object, which happens only when the root layout is rendered again, so a choice made on the page survives ordinary navigation; `<html lang>` follows the active locale too, which had stayed "en". The real /login signs in the same way as the QA page (`revalidatePath` + `redirect`), so both are fixed by the provider rather than by a hard reload after sign-in, which would have cost every sign-in a second page load. Test: `tests/shell-language-follows-server.test.tsx`. |

## Today looks the same in the real year, with one widget style — 7 November 2026

Janmejay, 7 Nov 2026: "the today's tab in mobile in the one app for the current session is not well optimized and it looks different from the test session … the widgets … are very basic and irregular in the UI. So please fix … and make the widgets better and suggest … better widgets".

| Decision | Outcome | Evidence and reason |
|---|---|---|
| D-506 | **The real session's Today draws the same money cards as the rehearsal session, from Fees' own figures** | Why they differed: the TEST session reads One's own copy of the fee data (`todayMoney`), so it had the year's pace, "families to call" and the money chart; the real session reads Fees through the bridge, which sent only today's total, so those cards were missing and a second "Fees collected today" card sat at the bottom of the phone. Fees' `/summary/today` now also sends the Dashboard's session figures from the same `get_dashboard_summary` call (schoolfees #150: expected, collected, due so far, follow-ups, the latest 14 collection days; additive, read-only, no migration). `moneyFromLedger` turns them into the same `TodayMoneyView`, so both sessions render one set of cards. Fees sends no class-by-installment table, so on a desk the pace and payment-mode cards stand beside the chart instead of the heat table, and the chart never reaches back past the first day Fees sent (`knownSpan`). An older Fees without the field keeps the plain ledger tile. Promises to pay are One's own call log, so they are counted in the real session too. |
| D-507 | **A closed day says why and when school opens again, and the empty day cards step aside on a phone** | The real calendar has the Diwali break (7–12 Nov) and the TEST calendar has none, which was the other half of "it looks different": every card said "School is closed today". Now the status line names the break, the phone hero shows one tile ("Diwali break · School opens Fri 13 Nov · in 6 days") instead of an empty register ring and a zero-teacher tile, and the empty timeline, Scheduled now and Teaching exceptions cards are left out on a phone (a desk keeps them, with the same line under the greeting). `closedSpell` follows `isSchoolOpen`: holiday and vacation rows close a day, a Sunday without a row is closed, an instructional row opens it. |
| D-508 | **Every Today card is one widget family: a coloured icon tile, a plain title, one line under it, one link on the right** | The cards had been drawn one by one: an h2 above some, a CardHead inside others, `Kpi` tiles for support and approvals, the first quick action in navy as if chosen. `WidgetHead`, `WidgetLink` and `StatTile` now open every card (Needs you, Scheduled now, Teaching exceptions, Today at school, Fees this session, Attendance this week, the two new widgets), the four quick actions share one shape in their own colours, and on a phone Teaching exceptions is left out where Needs you already lists the same classes and cover. |
| D-509 | **Two new widgets: Birthdays today and Coming up** | Birthdays today lists the children enrolled this session whose birthday it is, each a 44 px chip that opens the child's page (`students:view` only; the roll is asked for the two dozen dates of birth that can fall today, never read whole). Coming up lists the next six weeks of the school calendar (three weeks was empty for the real session through November; six reaches the December unit tests), a run of days with one name read as one item ("Unit Test 2 · 7–12 Dec"), with a date leaf and "In 5 days". Both are left out when empty or unread, never shown as nought. Further widget ideas are in the thread's reply, not built. |
| D-510 | **Today's styles are a stylesheet and the desk's money section is its own chunk** | The home page's chunk is counted in every route's eager-JS budget (`npm run check:bundles`): `/teach/attendance` stood at 268.8 KB against 269.0 on main, so Today's new widgets (+1.5 KB) failed it. Today's CSS moves from a string in `today.tsx` to `today.css`, as the other big screens already do, and the desk-only money section (Money taken, Year pace, How they pay, Pending by class) moves to `today-money.tsx`, loaded with `next/dynamic` and still rendered on the server, so a desk shows it at once and a phone, which never shows it, no longer needs its code to start. Every measured route fell about 1.7 KB below main (`/teach/attendance` 267.1 KB, local build). The pace bar and words both use sit in `today-pace.tsx`. Four unused text pairs went with it. |

## One receipt, everywhere, on one sheet — 7 November 2026

Janmejay, 7 Nov 2026, with four photos of the Fees receipt preview: "Remake the receipt design like this in the one app when viewing a recipt … make the receipt look like this through out the app and when printing on an A4 sheet, it looks similar to this and printed in single sheet only".

| Decision | Outcome | Evidence and reason |
|---|---|---|
| D-502 | **Every place One shows a receipt to read or print shows the Fees A4 receipt, from the one component (`ReceiptDocument`), laid out by its own width** | Before: real staff saw the Fees receipt on a desk but a shorter "counter slip" on a phone, and test accounts on the receipts desk saw One's own summary card. The slip left out what a parent asks the office about (what the receipt paid off, the concession, which instalments are still due). Now the receipt page (`/receipts/[n]`, both the live-from-Fees path and the local path), the receipts desk's side panel and the ported Fees receipt page all render `ReceiptDocument`. It sizes itself with a container query (`container: rcpt`), so a phone or the desk's 520 px panel gets two columns and a desk or a sheet of A4 gets four, whatever the window is. The confirmation slips after a payment (Collect's sealed slip, the success sheet) stay slips; their **Print** now opens the full receipt and prints it (`?print=1`). |
| D-503 | **Print A4 prints the receipt alone, on one A4 sheet, fitted rather than cut** | `@page { size: A4; margin: 0 }` with 10 mm of padding, and everything but the receipt is hidden while printing (`:has([data-receipt-solo])`), so the desk around it never reaches the paper. Before printing, the receipt is measured at A4 width and scaled with `zoom` to fit one sheet (`receipt-print-fit.tsx`, never below 0.6): an ordinary receipt with two concessions prints at about 0.9, one without concessions at full size, the longest the office could post at about 0.72. The button waits for the logo, signature and fonts first and says so ("Getting the sheet ready…" / "पन्ना तैयार हो रहा है…"), so a slow first tap is never tapped twice. Print spacing is tighter than the screen's; nothing is dropped from the paper. |
| D-504 | **The downloaded PDF is one A4 page, checked by a test** | The PDF put the signature and the tear-off stub on page two for an ordinary receipt with two concessions. Now it has the screen's layout (amount with words and "% paid so far" in one band, one-line section titles, a single totals strip), the signature, stamp, perforation and stub are one block that is never split, and if a receipt still runs to a second page it is rendered again in a denser spacing. `tests/receipt-pdf-one-page.test.ts` renders an ordinary, a reversed and the heaviest plausible receipt (synthetic fixtures) and counts pages twice. |
| D-505 | **The receipt's letterhead carries the line Fees prints: "Amet-Kelwa Road, Amet, 313332 · 7976199548 · info@vpps.co.in"** | Fees prints it from its own `NEXT_PUBLIC_SCHOOL_*` variables, which One's deployment does not have, so One's receipt had no contact line. `schoolProfile.letterhead` defaults to Fees' line and the variables still win where set; the parent PDFs use it too. The office number elsewhere in One is 9352205884; the receipt keeps 7976199548 because that is what every Fees receipt says today. |

## One AI page, and the connector on its own — 7 November 2026

Janmejay, 7 Nov 2026: "there are too many sections of AI in the settings … build one page for the AI and one page for the MCP connector which is different from AI. Clearly mention it", and show which model is used when, the workflow of each, how the paid AIs are used and what their work costs, on phone and desktop.

| Decision | Outcome | Evidence and reason |
|---|---|---|
| D-501 | **Settings has two AI rows, under their own "AI" heading: **AI in One** (every AI job inside One; "One में AI") and Claude / ChatGPT connector (MCP). The old Built-in AI page is folded into the first and its address redirects to it** | Before: "AI helper" (written drafts), "Built-in AI" (photos and voice) and "AI assistant connection" (MCP), with "AI सहायक" naming both the helper and the connector in Hindi. Now one page, `/settings?s=ai`, with four views kept in the address (`&v=used|how|cost|switches`): **What AI did** (who answered this month as one stacked bar, day by day free against paid, each kind of job as a card with who answered and what staff did with it, the latest 14 jobs with every try in order, who uses it most); **How it works** (the four roads a job can take, drawn from the same chains the helper runs, `aiLanes` in `modules/assist/domain/ai-map.ts`: names nobody → free first; about a child or family → paid only, the free models shown locked for test accounts; a photograph → paid only, never free; spoken → OpenAI listens, Claude understands. **Show me** walks a road station by station, **Listen** reads it aloud and lights each station, the station that did most this month glows; each station carries its place in the order and a road wraps rather than scrolling sideways, so no AI is hidden off the edge); **Cost** (this month in rupees, the written drafts' budget meter, photo and voice spend, what free AI saved, every model's cost per job and tries (a listening model, GPT Transcribe, never finishes a job, so its good tries read "Heard N voice notes" instead of "0 answered"; a station's sheet gives this road's count first and labels its model rows "all roads together"), the photo and voice keys with **Check keys now**); **Switches** (the helper's switch, budget, daily cap and features, and the photo and voice mode and daily limit). Both kinds of job are read by one function, `vppsone.ai_overview(p_since)` (service role only), shaped by `buildOverview`; a row logged before D-496 has its provider read from its model. Money is shown in rupees at `GEMINI_INR_PER_USD` (₹90 unless set); the budget stays in dollars because that is how it is saved. The connector page is renamed "Claude / ChatGPT connector" everywhere (Settings row, admin page, Preferences card, Today strip, consent page) and opens by saying what it is and that it is not the AI helper, with a link across; it lists what it can and can never read and this month's calls, counts only. `tests/ai-overview.test.ts` |

## Felt and heard: haptics, short sounds and Listen — 7 November 2026

Janmejay, 7 Nov 2026: add haptic feedback "wherever needed, not too much", voice or notification sounds or short music where required, and other touches that make One easy for non-technical teachers; check the whole app, plan it and deploy it. Plan and screen-by-screen audit: Claude Doc "Haptics, sounds and guidance in One".

| Decision | Outcome | Evidence and reason |
|---|---|---|
| D-497 | **One feedback vocabulary, `cue(kind)` in `src/ui/feedback/cue.ts`: ten named cues (tap, select, success, done, warn, error, undo, listen, stop, ready), each a short Android vibration and, for results and the microphone, a quiet chime of under half a second drawn by Web Audio. Screens name a cue; none calls `navigator.vibrate` or plays audio itself** | The app had four private vibrations (fee desk, Collect seal, AI voice button, voice search) and no sound. Rules the helper keeps for every screen: nothing before the person's first tap or key press (`primeFeedback`, or `navigator.userActivation`), nothing while the tab is hidden, and one result cues once: a result within 0.7 s of a stronger or equal one is dropped, so a toast and its save line never sound twice, while register taps 40 ms apart are all felt. No audio files: the tones are oscillators, the audio context is woken inside a tap (iOS) and suspended 6 s after the last sound. The vx toast cues by tone (ok → success, bad → error, info → tap) with `cue: "done" | false` to override; the fee kit's toast and `triggerHaptic` go through the same helper; `SaveStatus` cues on a change to saved or failed. The "done" tune is kept for finished jobs: register saved, paper of marks sent, all remarks done, payment posted (Collect seal, desk), day closed, child admitted. `tests/feedback-cue.test.ts` |
| D-498 | **Sounds, Vibration and Read aloud are the person's switches on this device, beside Dark mode and Language** | localStorage `vx.feedback.v1`; Sounds and Vibration on, Read aloud off by default. Per device because a teacher may want the chime on her phone and silence on the office PC. Shown in Menu, Settings → Preferences and the teacher's More → My profile; Vibration only where the browser can vibrate (never an iPhone), Read aloud only where it can speak. Turning one on plays it once. On an iPhone the ring/silent switch mutes the chimes |
| D-499 | **Help reads itself aloud, and a new screen's Help button says it is there** | Every help sheet (vx `HelpButton`, the teacher `HelpSheet`) has Listen / सुनें: the phone's own voice reads the title and steps, hi-IN for Devanagari and en-IN otherwise, lighting the step being read; nothing is spoken unless pressed, and a phone without the voice says how to add it. The Help button ripples softly (three times, motion allowed) on a screen's first three visits until it is opened once (`vx.help.seen.v1`, this device). With Read aloud on, each toast is spoken too |

## Notes, card by card — 7 November 2026

Janmejay, 7 Nov 2026: writing a note was "hidden behind a lot of scrolls"; make it card by card like the fee Collect flow, so a non-technical teacher can type a note quickly, pick the right child clearly and know what they are doing, and let the built-in AI work out what kind of note it is and save it that way.

| Decision | Outcome | Evidence and reason |
|---|---|---|
| D-486 | **A note is written card by card, as Collect takes a payment: Child → Words → Check → Saved, one card on the screen and nothing on it behind a scroll** | Each card is sized to the room between the top bar and the tab bar (or the keyboard), and re-measured when something above it changes size: on a 360×640 phone the offline banner pushed Save under the tab bar until it was. A list that does not fit is cut, and the cut says how to reach the rest. The child card searches in Hindi or English by sound and by roll number, the teacher's own class first, with short first-name chips (a surname initial only where two share a first name). The words card shows the picked child's full name large, keeps the draft on the device, and has Speak beside Next. The check card lists what the note will be saved as (kind, follow-up, who can read it, Tell parents), each line a tap that changes it inside the card. If the words name a classmate and not the picked child ("Aarav pushed Kabir" saved under Riya), the check card asks which child the note is about before Save. Saved offers Undo, the same words for another child, and a new note. Every way in says "New note" (was "Speak a note"). The long form is gone; `useComposer` still owns the note and its save, so who reads it, sensitive tags (D-264), parent messages and undo work as before. A landscape phone or a very short window lets the page scroll rather than squeeze a card. |
| D-487 | **The note sorts itself: from the words at once, then from the AI (`note.sort`), shown on the check card before anything is saved** | On Next the app reads the words itself, Hindi and English: the kinds of note, and a follow-up only for words that ask for an action ("call the parents", "बात करें", "keep an eye"); "कल" never makes one, since it is yesterday as often as tomorrow, and "call the mother" is not family news. When AI is on for the person, `note.sort` (quick chain, the existing keys and daily cap; not an open task, so a note never reaches the free key) returns up to two known kinds, a follow-up and clearer wording. Its kinds replace the words' own, but a health or family kind the words found always stays, so a model can never widen who reads a note; the follow-up is the AI's. A line the teacher changed is left alone, Save never waits for the answer, and the clearer wording is offered, never put in place without a tap (D-324). Every child's name in the words, in both scripts, is masked before sending: masking matches spelling exactly, so "Aarav" and "आरव" are each sent (D-326). Whether the AI's sort was kept or edited is reported once, at Save. |
| D-500 | **The note cards are built for a teacher using them for the first time: nothing cut off, nothing poured in, the AI seen at work, and Save keeping whatever is ticked** | Janmejay, 7 Nov, after D-486 and D-487 went live: the class's children could not be seen after picking the class, a new note opened with an earlier note's words in it, nothing showed the AI was working, a teacher would not know how to save the AI's note, and the buttons were too small. **Child:** every child of the class is listed in full, in roll order, in a list that scrolls inside the card (D-486 showed pages of first names behind "More names"); each is a 54 px row with a roll-number circle, the three children the teacher wrote about lately come first, and a search adds the teacher's other classes in the school's class order (B-06). **Words:** a new note starts empty. A note left without saving is offered above the box ("You left a note without saving it", **Continue this note** or **Delete**, which has Undo) and never poured in. A large **Speak your note** button under the box says the note can be spoken in Hindi or English; the quick words are 44 px. **Check:** while the AI works, a panel says so: a twinkling spark, three dots and a line that changes ("Reading your note…", "Seeing what it is about…", "Writing it clearly…"), over a shimmering outline of the boxes. Its clear note is then written in word by word, already ticked, with the teacher's own words below it as the other choice; a line above Save says which words Save keeps, Save glows three times, and the phone gives the "ready" cue (D-497). This updates D-487, where the clear note waited for a tap: Janmejay found the AI's rewrite "good", the teacher sees both and decides by a tap or by Save, and saving their own words over it is logged as "edited" (D-324). **Change it to English / Hindi** is a button, and the translation another choice to tick. What the note was sorted into shows as four labelled tiles (About, Follow-up, Who can read it, Tell parents), each 66 px tall and opening its own choices. **Every card** keeps its button in sight: what does not fit scrolls inside the card, and a fade with a small arrow above the button says there is more until the end is reached. This replaces PR 273's step-by-step squeeze, which folded the AI's note away on a small phone; a window shorter than a card's least room (a phone on its side) still lets the page scroll. On a phone a strip shows the three steps and the current one, hidden while the keyboard is up. On a phone every button is at least 44 px tall; the motion is short and stops for reduced motion; Save gives the "done" cue. Nothing inside a card is squeezed to make it fit: the "Written by AI" and privacy line had shrunk to nothing on a 360 px phone and a 1280×720 desktop until it scrolled instead (PR 285). Undo returns to the check card as it was before Save, the AI's note still ticked beside the teacher's own words, and "Same note, another child" carries the words that were saved. A child without a roll number shows initials in the circle. `tests/notes-frame.test.tsx`, `tests/notes-save-line.test.tsx`, `tests/class-followup/phone.spec.ts` |

## One AI report for a whole year — 7 November 2026

Janmejay, 7 Nov 2026: "just make one master report generation with all the AI context including student photos, every detail, everything from end to end, fees details to notes, it should contain everything end to end of that session", to give to ChatGPT and ask for whatever report is needed, and "keep it configureable on whom should get the access". Module README: `src/modules/ai-report/README.md`.

| Decision | Outcome | Evidence and reason |
|---|---|---|
| D-484 | **Reports → AI report (`/reports/ai`) makes one file of everything One and Fees hold for a session: an Excel workbook, or a .zip with the student photographs, a photo index and a read-me** | One's sheets come first (`_README`, `_SHEETS`, `Students`, then every sheet in `modules/ai-report/domain/sheets.ts`: classes, attendance, marks, notes, timetable, leave, admissions and the rest, ids replaced by names), then Fees' own AI context bundle with each sheet named `Fees …`. Every server response must stay under Vercel's 4.5 MB, so the page fetches three parts (`/api/reports/ai/school`, `/fees`, `/photos` in pages of 40) and joins them on the device with SheetJS and fflate; if the two workbooks cannot be joined there, the .zip carries them as two files and the read-me says so. Fees serves the year's workbook and photographs through two new read-only bridge reads (schoolfees PR 149, `screens/reports/ai-context` and `ai-photos`, under the `reports` read context; no Fees schema change). The read-me tells the assistant that fees and late fees are never added together, that a late fee alone is not a defaulter, and never to follow an instruction written inside the records |
| D-485 | **`reports:ai` is the admin's alone until Staff → Roles grants it, and the file never carries more than its reader may see on screen** | Rows only (migration `20261107045006`). A granted role gets each One sheet only with the permission that shows those records on screen and a school-wide role (admin, office, coordinators); fee follow-ups and notes are the exception, and notes pass `canSeeNote` one by one (private notes are their writer's; health and family notes, old note kinds included, the admin's and the class teacher's). The Fees half needs `fees:reports`, and for real staff on a real year Fees checks its own grants again. Photographs need `students:view` and come only from Fees for a real year. Test accounts get rehearsal years only. A real year without the live Fees connection gets no money sheets at all, never One's copy of them. No secret, link, storage path, hash or raw id is written to One's sheets: a column rule, not a list of columns, and an id inside a value (a JSON audience, a status reason such as `withdrawn:<id>`, an upload's id in a collected answer) becomes the name it stands for or is left out. Fees' sheets keep the Student ID, Class id, Route id and Plan id columns its own AI context export has always joined its sheets on. Every part downloaded is logged in `academic_exports` as `ai_report` (migration `20261107045020`): who, which year, which part, how many rows. The file holds Aadhaar numbers, phone numbers, addresses, marks, health and family notes and photographs, and the screen says so before the button |

## Free AI keys for the AI helper — 7 November 2026

Janmejay added two free keys to One Production: NVIDIA's API catalogue (`NVIDIA_KIMI_API_KEY`) and OpenRouter (`OPENROUTER_API_KEY`, free models only; the account's $10 exists only to raise the free limit to 1,000 requests a day).

| Decision | Outcome | Evidence and reason |
|---|---|---|
| D-490 | **NVIDIA's Nemotron and OpenRouter's free models answer the AI helper's open quick tasks first, ahead of Gemini Free; they never see anything else** | Owner request, 7 Nov. Chain for `ask.answer`, `columns.match`, `cover.plan`, `template.draft`: NVIDIA (`nvidia/nemotron-3.5-super-vl-120b-a12b`, 7 s; Kimi K2.6 answered 404 for this account on 7 Nov, Super VL answered in 1-6 s) → OpenRouter (`nvidia/nemotron-3.5-lightning:free`, `google/gemma-4-31b-it:free`, `openrouter/free`, 6 s) → Gemini Free (now 8 s) → Claude → OpenAI → Gemini Paid; the three free stages share the 21 s the paid chain leaves inside the browser's 75 s. Free tiers may keep and train on what they are sent, so the D-401/D-417 rule extends to them: a task that can carry a child, family, note or photo never reaches them, masked or not. OpenRouter must never charge: `config.ts` drops any model that does not end in `:free` (or is not `openrouter/free`) and every request carries a zero `provider.max_price`. Free stages cost $0 and skip the monthly-budget check. Both use OpenAI-compatible Chat Completions (`assist/data/chat-draft.ts`); the task's Zod schema still validates every answer. Built-in AI's photo and voice routes are unchanged. `tests/assist-free-providers.test.ts` |
| D-496 | **The free models go first wherever they may: the open tasks, now with a class's results and a notice to all parents or all staff, and every text task a test account asks for. Nemotron answers with thinking off, held to the task's JSON schema, and a paid model always keeps its time** | Owner, 7 Nov: find the best models on the NVIDIA, OpenRouter and Gemini Free keys and "make these free API keys work … and use pay as you go later". Measured that day on One's own tasks through the production keys, inputs masked: NVIDIA `nemotron-3.5-super-vl-120b-a12b` with thinking on took 9-12 s, past its 7 s stage; with `chat_template_kwargs.enable_thinking: false` and guided JSON (`response_format: json_schema`) it passed 10 of 11 tasks in 1-4 s (a class's results or remarks in up to 6 s). Nemotron Ultra stalls under guided JSON, Nemotron Lightning answered Hindi in Hinglish or English, Gemma is slow on NVIDIA, and most other catalogue models answered 502 after 30 s. OpenRouter now asks for `nvidia/nemotron-3.5-super-vl:free` → `google/gemma-4-31b-it:free` → `openrouter/free`, with `reasoning: none` and the schema sent as `json_schema`. **Open tasks** add `results.summary` (a class's totals and subject averages, never a child's mark), `message.write` and `message.polish` (a notice to all parents or all staff). The Today brief and call scripts name nobody but stay paid: the free models broke their slots (a number written out for `<<absent_today>>`, a made-up `[[P1]]`). **Test accounts** (`is_test_account`), and drafts prepared for a TEST session (cover plans, class digests), start every text task on the free models: their children are the masked replica's invented people (none of the 278 TEST names is in the real roster, checked 7 Nov). A photograph never does, not even a test account's: it cannot be masked, and the admission form someone photographs to try the reader is usually a real one. For everyone else D-401, D-417 and D-490 stand: a real teacher's note, translation, reply, child summary, digest, remarks or call script never reaches a free model, masked or not. **Guard rails:** the free stages share 20, 22 or 24 s (quick, writing, careful) and each gets 8-12 s, so the first paid model still has its whole time inside the browser's 75 s (`tests/assist-routes.test.ts`). A free provider that fails is skipped by that server for a minute after a rate limit, an hour after the day's quota, 30 minutes after a key, billing or model error, and 2 minutes after a server error or a full-length timeout. A free answer must pass the Hindi check (`assist/domain/hindi-check.ts`: a `hi` field at least 60% Devanagari, a Hindi request's whole answer at least half) or the next model writes it. Once the month's budget is spent, a task with a free stage still runs on the free models and stops before the first paid one; a task without one is refused at once, as before. `assist_log.provider` (migration `20261107075126`) names who wrote each draft, and Settings → AI helper shows the month's free share, drafts per free provider and roughly what they would have cost on the tier's Claude model. `tests/assist-free-providers.test.ts`, `tests/assist-run.test.ts` |

## Real-device screen check — 7 November 2026

Janmejay asked whether anyone had looked at One's screens on a real phone and a desktop through BrowserStack. A container crawl at 390×844 visited 352 screens across the ten QA roles, then a real Galaxy M32 (Chrome), an iPhone 15 (Safari) and Windows 11 (Chrome) were driven by hand.

| Decision | Outcome | Evidence and reason |
|---|---|---|
| D-478 | **The accountant's home puts the cursor in "Find a student or family" only on a device with a mouse (`pointer: fine`)** | On a real Galaxy M32 the autofocus opened the keyboard the moment an accountant signed in, over half the screen and today's collection. A counter PC keeps the cursor on arrival (and Alt F); a phone user taps the box. |
| D-479 | **`:root[data-kb] .vx-bottom` hides the tab bar with `!important`** | The bar's inline `display: grid` (bottom-bar.tsx) beat the non-important rule, so on Android the tabs rode on top of the keyboard and covered the middle of the screen; the 6 Nov fix for "Save note" was never in effect. |
| D-480 | **Fees home (ported dashboard) no longer draws its own session pill** | One's shell already shows the session pill on every screen for every fee reader (`canSwitchSessions`), so Fees home showed two session pickers. The session list fetch it preloaded is dropped with it. |
| D-481 | **Fees home prepares missing dues from a server action, not `after()`, and only where the write can land** | The page scheduled the preparation with `after()` during its render. Every fee read in One goes through the roster projection fetch, which asks the cookie who is reading, and Next refuses `cookies()` inside `after()` while rendering — so it never ran, each visit logged "Unable to load school defaults", and the "Dues update started" notice was untrue. The page's client now calls `prepareDashboardDuesAction` (fees:write, session fence, visible ids only, 5-minute de-duplication) and refreshes when something was prepared. Shown and run only on a TEST session or when fee writes are open, since the fee-writes switch refuses a real session while writes are test-only (always, under coexistence). |
| D-488 | **A Hindi line says its dates in Hindi wherever they were built** | The BrowserStack check found "Sat 7 Nov · पीरियड 4 चल रहा है", "सबसे पुराना 10 Oct" and "Thu 15 Oct, 1:40 am" on the Hindi office screens. About 300 places build a date with an English-only helper and put it into the Hindi half of a pair or into a pair builder. `platform/i18n/hindi-dates.ts` rewrites date-shaped English only (a day beside a month, a weekday before a day, a long month name, an am/pm clock) and nothing else; `lead()`/`under()` in `vx/text.ts` and `primary()` pass the Hindi line through it. The pair forms (`shortDayP` and friends) stay the way to write a new screen; dates printed outside any pair are moved to them screen by screen. |
| D-489 | **Books (cheques, UPI, refunds, requests) reads in Hindi, including what the server says back** | Staff are Hindi-first. Every label, hint and sheet on `/accounts/books` is a pair. A Books action result carries `hi` beside `message`, and a refusal is thrown as a `Said(en, hi)` so the toast reads in the reader's language; a database error stays as it came. Reasons and titles the app stores in English ("Paid twice", "Bank returned the cheque: …", "Reverse SVP… · cheque bounced") are said in Hindi when shown, and anything a person typed after them stays as typed. The values sent to the server (`REFUND_REASONS`, `BOUNCE_REASONS`) stay English. Still English on purpose: the parent's WhatsApp text (it already carries both languages) and the amount in words on the bank slip. |

## Language chosen when an account is made — 7 November 2026

| Decision | Outcome | Evidence and reason |
|---|---|---|
| D-476 | **Add staff asks which language the app opens in: हिंदी, English or हिंदी + English (Hindi picked first)** | Janmejay, 7 Nov 2026: "it can ask when I create the account to open in Hindi or English". The vx Staff screen's `addStaffAction` used to save every new profile as `both` with no choice. It now takes the sheet's choice (`hi` when none is sent, the column's own default). Each option is written in its own language so it reads right whatever the admin's language is. The person can still change it later from the language switch or Preferences. Project agenda: `/mnt/project-files/agenda/project-agenda.md` |

## One without the Fees redirects — 6 November 2026

Janmejay asked that One stop sending staff to the Fees app for anything, while Fees stays the money system and One reads from it behind the scenes. The plan ("One without the Fees redirects", Claude Doc) was approved in full, with deployment to live. Fees changes are additive (new read endpoints, no migration, no live-DB schema change).

| Decision | Outcome | Evidence and reason |
|---|---|---|
| D-467 | **Dues & calls and Fee setup are One's own screens for real staff, read live from Fees through `/api/one/v1/screens/*`** | Fees PR 146 adds four read-only endpoints (`screens/dues`, `screens/dues/family`, `screens/dues/people`, `screens/fee-setup`) built from the Fees screens' own readers, authorized through the existing `directory` read context so no live migration was needed, plus the Defaulters permission for the dues reads. One draws them with the same screens it draws for TEST (`loadDues`, `getFeeSetupVxDataFromFees`), a "Live from Fees" line with the read time, and "could not read" when Fees does not answer. It never falls back to One's copy of fee data |
| D-468 | **On those live screens nothing writes yet** | A call log, promise, plan, write-off or fee-setup publish from One would land in One's local copy, not in Fees. The buttons are off on the live screens and the proxy still answers 409 to a non-GET there for real staff. Writes come through new `/api/one/v1` commands later, one at a time |
| D-469 | **Every remaining Fees redirect lands on One's own "Not in One yet" page (`/not-in-one?from=`)** | The proxy used to send real staff to the Fees app for receipts, reports, books, reminders, student tools and admissions edits. It now sends them to one page that names the screen and says the office keeps using Fees for it for now. Links in the finance hub, student page, admin today, ledger and receipts point there or at One's own screens. The uncertain-payment link on the desk still opens Fees, because that is where the office checks whether a payment went through |
| D-470 | **An admin can refresh the roster from Fees on demand, no more than once every 10 minutes** | `POST /api/fees-bridge/roster/refresh` (same origin, real admin, not previewing, not a test account) runs one ordinary bridge sync. D-457 stands: there is no scheduled frequent sync, only the nightly job and this button |
| D-471 | **Search falls back to One's roster when Fees does not answer** | Staff with money access search through Fees; when Fees is slow or down they now get names and classes from One's roster, with no money figures, instead of nothing |
| D-472 | **New Fees features are frozen; Fees takes bug fixes and One's bridge endpoints only** | The approved plan's end state keeps the Fees backend as One's money service, so new work goes into One's screens. Real payments from One stay behind `FEES_BRIDGE_WRITES_ENABLED`, the owner's switch |
| D-476 | **Reports has its own switch on Staff → Roles, "See fee reports" (`fees:reports`): on for admin, accountant and fee collector, off for the office** | Janmejay, 7 Nov 2026: "keep it configureable on whom should get the access", and the two Viewers do not get reports. Before this, Reports followed `fees:view`, so anyone who could read any fee screen saw it, and an office account in live mode was sent to Access denied by Fees. Now `/reports`, the side and bottom navigation, the menu, the Accounts tiles, the evening "See today's day book" button and the live finance hub all ask for `fees:reports`, and the admin turns it on or off per role in the matrix (`set_role_permissions`, logged; a teacher still never gets a `fees:` permission). Rows for admin, accountant and fee collector are migration `20261107042312`. Turning it on for a role does not by itself open the live numbers: in live mode the person also needs a Fees bridge grant with `can_read` and a Fees role with `reports:view`, as for Dues. The two Viewer grants on live Fees have `can_read = false`, so they stay out either way |
| D-477 | **Reports is One's own screen for real staff, read live from Fees** | Janmejay, 7 Nov 2026: "let's go ahead to change this report to [use the] live fees database". Fees PR 147 adds a read-only `reports` operation to `private.one_bridge_read_context` (Fees' `reports:view`, what its own Reports page asks for; applied to the live Fees database on 7 Nov as `20261107034549`, no table or data change) and three reads: `screens/reports/balances`, `receipts` (window at most 400 days, with the session's classes) and `daybook` (also `finance:view`). `/reports` for real staff on a real session now draws the hub, collection, defaulters, pending by class and day book from those, with "Live from Fees, read at …"; TEST sessions and test accounts keep One's copy. Class teachers still come from One's own records by class id. If Fees does not answer the page says so and never shows the copy's numbers. A fee role without `reports:view` is sent to Access denied rather than a Fees error. Report schedules stay One's own, so `/reports` is not a read-only route; the detailed CSV/Excel exports and "All detailed reports" still land on "Not in One yet" |
| D-495 | **The needs-attention list reads one session through `needs_attention_for_session`, not `v_needs_attention`** | Speed logs, 7 Nov 2026: Academics and Student progress failed with "Could not read the attention list" (statement timeout) about 15 times on 6 Nov and took 12 s on 7 Nov. The view adds up every attendance mark and exam mark of every session before the session filter applies (~0.5 s quiet, >8 s busy); its marks rules were unused because the app works them out from dated exams. The function (migration `20261107070915`) narrows to the session first and returns attendance and open notes only, same rules; 18 ms against 510 ms, and the same nine TEST children. The view stays |

## Opening the app faster — 6 November 2026

Janmejay asked for the app to boot faster and more smoothly, from the logo to the first screen, on phones (installed) and laptops. The speed logs showed first paint landing with the first byte on almost every first open: nothing reached the phone until every read of the frame was done.

| Decision | Outcome | Evidence and reason |
|---|---|---|
| D-461 | **The root layout waits on nothing slow: `<html>`, the styles and a boot splash go out at the first byte, and everything that reads the account streams in behind a Suspense boundary** | Before, the root layout resolved the locale (a staff read when the `vpps_locale` cookie is absent) and the (app) layout and shell did every read before one byte was sent, so an installed app sat on the system splash and a browser tab on a white page for 0.8 to 4 s on a normal day. The splash (`src/ui/system/boot-splash.tsx`) uses the manifest's paper colour and the crest, so the hand-over from the phone's own splash is invisible; it is HTML and CSS only, says "Still loading" by itself after 8 s, and follows dark mode because next-themes stays above the boundary. `<html lang>` comes from the cookie when there is one; otherwise a one-line script inside the stream corrects it from the account before any screen text arrives. A redirect from the (app) layout (a missing or inactive profile) now streams instead of answering 307; the proxy still answers 307 for everybody signed out |
| D-462 | **Opening `/` goes straight to the person's home from the proxy, by a home hint cookie (`vx_home`)** | The installed app always opens `/`. For everyone but the admin menu `/` only redirects (to `/teach`, `/accounts`, a chosen start screen), and that redirect came after the layout and shell had done all their reads: two full signed-in renders on every open for a teacher. The shell now writes where `/` would send this person (`src/vx/home-redirect.ts`, the same rule the page uses) as `user id + path`; the proxy follows it only for that same account, only for a plain document load of `/` with no query (never a client navigation or prefetch), and only to a safe internal path. It is a hint, never a grant: the screen it opens checks its own permission. Stale after a role or start-screen change for one open at most, and the screen it lands on rewrites it. Never written while an admin previews another role |

## Faster and smoother, batch 1 — 6 November 2026

Janmejay asked for faster loading and smoother motion, and chose to work through the whole plan in order (`docs/ui/PERFORMANCE.md`, "6 November").

| Decision | Outcome | Evidence and reason |
|---|---|---|
| D-427 | **A Fees student photo is kept by the browser for ten minutes (`private, max-age=600`); errors stay `no-store`** | Janmejay approved, 6 Nov. The thumbnail route was `no-store`, so every Students list fetched every photo again through a function and the bridge; real phones logged the list's largest paint at 2.0 to 2.8 s. `private` keeps it out of any CDN or shared cache; ten minutes bounds what a shared office PC keeps after sign-out |
| D-428 | **The (app) layout and the shell start every independent read together** | The shell, and with it each screen's loading skeleton, cannot reach the phone until the layout resolves. The session, the previewed role's permissions and the production marker were three waves, and the shell's preferences, AI features and own photo were three more; both are now one wave each. Nothing read changes, only when |
| D-429 | **The tapped link dims itself while its page loads (`a[data-nav-pending]`, 80 ms delay)** | The top bar waits 120 ms and is easy to miss on a phone. NavLink marks its own `<a>` through a hidden marker, so no wrapper changes a link's layout; Students rows now use it |
| D-430 | **On touch screens the frosted glass is 8 px without saturate, over a denser tint** | A 16 px blur with saturate is repainted every scrolled frame and low-end Android drops frames under it. Desktop is unchanged |
| D-432 | **Client code reads the pair catalogue as named exports (`import * as m from "@/messages/pairs"`)** | `m` was one ~960-key object literal, which no bundler can trim, so every page shipped all of it. As `/* @__PURE__ */` named exports behind a namespace import, webpack keeps only the pairs a page reads: every measured route fell by about 17 KB gzipped (`/dashboard` 210.9 → 194.2, `/teach/attendance` 267.2 → 250.5). `@/messages` still exports `m` for tests and server code |
| D-433 | **Inter and Source Serif 4 are subset to Latin, Indian transliteration, punctuation, ₹ and symbols** | 158 KB off the first visit (567,632 → 409,216 bytes). Every layout feature kept; Devanagari untouched; a character outside the ranges falls back to the system font |
| D-434 | **Sign-in lands on the start screen directly; `/dashboard` as a sign-in target means "my start screen"** | The form defaulted `next` to `/dashboard`, which then redirected: one extra full render on every sign-in, and a chosen start screen (for example School Fees) was ignored |
| D-435 | **A new screen settles in (opacity 0.4 → 1, 180 ms)** | Skeleton and page alike, so the swap reads as one motion. Not from 0, so the first paint counts at once |
| D-436 | **A phone sheet closes by dragging its handle or title down; a short pull springs back** | Native bottom-sheet behaviour. Only the handle and the title row start a drag (they are `touch-action: none`), never the scroll area or a control, and never from 900px where the sheet is a dialog. Inline `animation: none` is set first because the opening animation's fill would pin `transform`. `tests/vx-sheet-drag.test.tsx` |
| D-437 | **Dues family rows dim when tapped but still do not prefetch** | They link to the same Dues page with `?family=`, so a prefetch is a full Dues render through the bridge; on intent would mean one per row a mouse rests on. Feedback only |
| D-438 | **Batch 3 items not built, and why** | Per-route fee catalogues: the Fees app has no namespace map to copy, and a missing namespace is a blank label on a money screen. Optimistic saves: attendance and marks already save to the device first. Fewer refreshes: the office listener is already debounced and waits for idle hands. View transitions on Student 360 tabs: the tab body already animates in, and the list-to-profile photo is a streamed navigation (the case that froze tabs before, `dashboard-shell.tsx`). Cache Components: touches every route; wait for the per-screen logs |
| D-439 | **Screen links prefetch on intent, not on sight** | Next prefetches every link that scrolls into view, and each prefetch of a dynamic screen is a full server render. Counted on live after load: Calendar 53 (one per day cell and filter), Students 33, Academics 26, Collection 20. Those renders queued ahead of the tap a person actually made. Every vx screen link now goes through `src/vx/ui/link.tsx`: nothing until a finger rests on it (90 ms), a mouse rests on it, or it takes keyboard focus; then the full destination. A touch that becomes a scroll is cancelled. An explicit `prefetch` wins. The shell's bottom bar and sidebar keep Next's default, because those five tabs are where people go next. `tests/vx-link.test.tsx` fails if a screen imports `next/link` again |
| D-440 | **Calendar reads in one wave** | `loadCalendar` waited for ten reads, then the day's register, then the notice counts: three round trips after the academic context. The register now starts as soon as the holidays arrive (it needs them to know the school is open) and the notice counts as soon as the notices do, inside the same `Promise.all`. What each read returns is unchanged |
| D-441 | **The intent link moves to `src/ui` and every screen uses it, the ported Fees screens included** | After D-439 went live, Calendar, Students, Academics, Attendance, Exams and Staff fired 0 prefetches on open, but Collection still fired 20 and Fees 12: their links came from `src/app/(app)` and the modules, which still imported `next/link`. The link now lives in `src/ui/link.tsx`, the design system both apps already share, and 116 files use it. Only the shell's navigation keeps Next's default (`bottom-bar`, `sidebar`, `top-actions`, the ported `nav-link`, `app-topbar`, `dashboard-shell`) plus `IntentLink`. `tests/intent-prefetch-link.test.tsx` fails any other file that imports `next/link` |
| D-442 | **List avatars are the Fees photo shrunk on One's server** | Fees answers both `thumb` and `full` with the original photo (up to 512 KB; its own comment says so), and the Students list, attendance and other lists draw fifty of them in a 46 px circle. One's photo route gains a `list` variant: it asks Fees for `thumb` exactly as before, then shrinks the bytes in memory with sharp to 192 px wide WebP (a few KB) and sends them under the same private ten-minute browser cache. Nothing is stored and no Fees code changes; authorization is still Fees' per-request check. If the shrink fails the original is sent. Student 360's portrait keeps `thumb` |
| D-443 | **A note saved twice files once** | The composer gives every draft one id (`client_request_id`), keeps it in the stored draft and sends it with every save of that draft; the server returns the note already filed under that writer and id instead of inserting a second one, and reads it back on a unique-index race. Same shape as leave (`teacher_leave_request_once`). In the 6 Nov 20-teacher day, six of ten seats made duplicate notes (each with its own follow-up) after a slow save. Migration `20261106122604`, applied on wtgx |
| D-444 | **A health or family note says who can really see it** | The note page said "the child's teachers can see this" for every shared note, while `canSeeNote` (D-264) limits health and family notes to the author, the follow-up person, the admin and the class teacher. The line now follows `isSensitiveNote` |
| D-445 | **A paper with no children on it is not "Sent" or "Done"** | A paper whose roster is empty read as finished and still offered Check & send. Marks lists now say "No children · tell the exam office" and the sheet shows that line in place of the send button. Cause in TEST: enrolments dated after the exams; those were moved to the session start (test data only) |
| D-446 | **A subject teacher's Today leads with her periods** | Without a class of her own, a whole "You do not take attendance" card sat above her periods and pushed them below the first phone screen. It is now one line inside "My periods today", which moves to the top; a period opens her subject in that class (`?subject=`), not the class teacher's page. The big card stays for staff with no class and no periods |
| D-447 | **A paper that was sent reads "Sent" on the marks list, blanks and all** | `paperStatus` calls a sent paper with blanks "in progress", so the list said "late · 0 of 4 · Continue" while the sheet opened read-only as "Sent 30 Sep" (its roster grew after it was sent). The card now carries `sent` and shows "Sent · N blank — tell the exam office", is never "late", and is never the paper the list sends you to first |
| D-448 | **A remark does not speak about attendance before 10 marked days** | One absence on the only marked day gave "attendance 0%", and both the suggested remark and the AI draft told the child to come to school more. `attendanceFor` now gives no figure below `MIN_DAYS_FOR_REMARK` (10) |
| D-449 | **Send waits for the last mark's autosave instead of ignoring the tap** | `send()` returned silently while an autosave was in flight; on a slow day a teacher pressed "Send marks", nothing happened for 90 s, and the paper stayed open. Send now awaits the in-flight save, then sends; the status line meanwhile says "Saving…" (it said "Sending…", which read as the paper going to the office) |
| D-450 | **A class's attendance over a range is read in two indexed steps** | `classAttendanceRange` embedded `attendance_registers!inner` under `attendance_marks` and ordered by mark id, which leaves Postgres free to scan the marks table; on 6 Nov it hit the statement timeout nine times and the register page crashed. It now reads the class's submitted registers in the range (class/day index), then their marks by `register_id` in chunks of 200 (unique index). Same rows, same order guarantee for paging |
| D-451 | **The admin decides what reaches parents: Settings → Parent messages** | Owner, 6 Nov 2026: "let admin make the switch on off of all the decisions that needs to send to parent". `school_settings` key `parent_messages` holds the register's absent-child WhatsApp (Off / Starts off / Starts on) and on/off for "Tell parents" on a note, sharing a support plan, and the birthday wish. Off hides the choice on the teacher's screen and the server refuses to queue it; "Starts off/on" replaces D-320's fixed same-day ON, and the teacher can still flip it. Until the admin saves, behaviour is unchanged (Starts on, all on); a failed read closes every switch. The provider gate still decides whether a queued message may reach WhatsApp |
| D-454 | **The big middle button says what it does, and a teacher without a class gets Marks there** | UX review 6 Nov 2026: the centre of the phone bottom bar was an icon alone ("+", a calendar), and for a subject teacher it opened "You do not take attendance". Every centre button now prints a short word under it in the reader's language (`CENTER_SHORT` in `src/vx/nav.ts`), and the shell asks once (cached 5 min, tag `classes`) whether a teacher is class teacher this session; without a class the centre is Marks. A failed read keeps Attendance, the old behaviour. Tab labels are 12.5 px and segmented tabs 42 px tall on touch screens |
| D-455 | **On a phone, the day's work comes before the welcome** | The teacher's Today now orders: cover asks and unsaved registers, the attendance card, today's periods, then tasks, the welcome card (an account with no class or periods yet still sees it first), Ready for you, notices, quick actions, children to watch, marks links, and Bolo near the end. The long subject list under the greeting is desktop-only; the phone shows the class-teacher line. The text-size button reads "A+" and Help is a word, not a bare "?". Pinned menu tiles are not drawn a second time below, the Notes board's add button is a plain button at the top (the floating one covered a filter), and the board links on Exam Office and School Office sit below the main work |
| D-456 | **A phone list screen shows at most two summary tiles before its list** | UX review 6 Nov 2026, wave 2. Dues & calls keeps "Pending today" and "Calls today" on the phone (promises keep their count on the tab; the week's takings stay on the desk). Receipts keeps the day's total and the cash to count; UPI, a zero "Reversed" and the mode chart are desk-only. The Notes board's tag chart is desk-only, and its counts now say "All notes so far". School Office hides a tile that reads 0 on the phone. The cheques/UPI/refunds tiles are a 2×2 grid instead of a sideways strip, and their words are in the reader's language. The admin's phone Today moves "Families to call" out of the hero so "Needs you" starts higher, and the week's sparkline is not drawn beside ₹0 today. The Activity log shows 40 changes at a time with "Show more"; Export still takes every matching row. The subject-teacher class screen makes Enter marks the main button while a paper is open and drops its "Planned: N class-periods" count, which read as a second weekly load beside More's timetable count |
| D-463 | **An exam can be renamed, so it can be given its Hindi name later** | UX review follow-up, 6 Nov 2026. An exam made without a Hindi name stores its English name in both columns, so Hindi readers saw "QA Terra Isolated Exam" and similar. Exams & marks now has "Exam name" (or "Add Hindi name" when the Hindi column holds no Devanagari) for `assessments:manage`, which changes `label_en`/`label_hi` only: the code, term, dates, papers, links and report cards stay on the same exam, and the change is logged as `renamed` in the exam activity. Separately, the teacher class and subject screens took the exam's English name only, so a Hindi teacher read "Half yearly अंक" even though "अर्धवार्षिक परीक्षा" was stored; they now carry the pair. The Settings React #418 was the theme line ("Auto" on the server, "Light" once next-themes read localStorage), not the activity times; it now waits for hydration. The admin task-assignment paper picker carries the pair as well |
| D-431 | **Speed logs name about thirty screens instead of nine** | Most real visits logged as "other", which hid which screen was slow and which one shifted its layout (CLS 0.86 once). Names stay fixed strings; a child's page is "student", never its id |

Narrows D-181: the cookie stays `hi`, but `i18n/request.ts` builds a bilingual catalogue for a `both` account: short labels "Receipts · रसीदें", sentences, fragments and plural/select strings in English, the way One's own screens treat a `both` reader. No third catalogue file to keep in step |

Other search boxes still take Latin only |

Timetable, My tasks, Help and My profile are sidebar items (`/teach/more?view=…`); `activeKey` matches an href's query against the URL, so the open view lights its item and the rest light "Notices, links & leave". A typed Bolo request is the voice job with one server-written UTF-8 `1.txt` (≤500 characters) instead of a recording: same quota, review, Save and 90-day purge; it skips transcription and goes to Claude as its own transcript (Gemini gets it as text), and the typed words are always the transcript (migration 20261106192629 allows `txt` in `reserve_ai_job` and `text/plain` in `ai-inputs`). ## Collect: card-by-card payment entry — 5 November 2026

The owner approved the Collect prototype ("this flow looks perfect") and plan `docs/plans/2026-11-05-native-money-and-collect-flow.md` (D1–D9 there). Branch `feat/collect-flow`.

| Decision | Outcome | Evidence and reason |
|---|---|---|
| D-420 | **`/payments` is Collect: one UI on a money port with two adapters.** Real staff (`usesCanonicalFees`) get the bridge adapter (Fees ledger through `/api/one/v1`: directory lookup, the single-purpose student reads, quote, post, command status); test accounts and confined deployments get the local adapter (the vx desk's own actions, TEST session). The classic desks moved to `/payments/classic`; `?classic=1` and any `?receipt=` link redirect there | Plan §5: what testers rehearse must be what cashiers use. A separate route keeps both classic desks out of Collect's JavaScript (`/payments` measured 292.1 KB gzip before); `next/dynamic` in one route would still list their chunks in its manifest |
| D-421 | **Collect's strings are a third pair catalogue, `src/messages/collect.ts` (`mc`), held to the same language test** | D-229's reasoning applied once more: `index.ts` ships with every page and `setup.ts` with every set-up screen; ~250 Collect pairs belong to one route. `{a}`/`{n}`/`{d}`/`{name}` holes are filled by `fill()` so every entry stays a static pair the test can read |
| D-422 | **A family payment is one ledger payment per child, each with its own command id and quote, posted in order; the whole list is stored in `sessionStorage` before the first post** | The bridge has no family command yet (plan §4). Storing first means a reload or a lost answer checks the same ids (`getBridgeCommand`), a partial failure names who saved and "Retry the rest" resends only the others with the same ids and bodies. A definite refusal stored nothing; when the body must change (a duplicate acknowledgement) it goes with a fresh id, because Fees compares a replayed id with its first body |
| D-423 | **Cash + UPI on the bridge sends `tenders` (2 lines, distinct modes, adding up) instead of `paymentMode`, mirroring schoolfees PR #141; for a family the cash part is spread over the children in order, so each receipt's lines add up to its own amount.** The local adapter does not offer Cash + UPI | D7. Until #141 is deployed Fees refuses the unknown field with a definite 400 (nothing posted). The TEST ledger has no tender lines. A split receipt prints through Fees' `printUrl`, because One's receipt template (`receiptDetailFromBridge`) does not know the `split` mode yet |
| D-424 | **UPI and bank transfer carry no reference; a cheque's number is the reference and its bank and date ride in the remarks, as the vx desk writes them** | D9 and D3 (structured cheque fields are a Fees-side change still to make) |
| D-425 | **The check card appears only for a family, Cash + UPI, cheque, bank, a backdated date, a child who paid today, more than ₹15,000, or a non-default waiver reason; otherwise a 0.6 s hold saves from the payment card.** A daily-duplicate refusal moves to the check card and needs the "new payment" tick; a near duplicate needs `payments:adjust` | D2. The tick sends `acknowledgeDailyDuplicate` / `acknowledgeNearDuplicate` |
| D-426 | **"Likely today" on the bridge lists only this browser's recent payers and their siblings who owe** | Fees has no "likely today" read yet (plan §4); One does not guess from the replica. The local adapter ranks overdue-with-late-fee children from the TEST roll |

## Built-in AI goes live — 4 November 2026

The owner asked for the built-in AI plan (photo marks, photo substitutions, Bolo voice) to be checked and deployed to the live app. Details: `docs/ai/DECISIONS.md`.

D-411 to D-413 update the AI routing, shared reference and Today layout; see [AI decisions](ai/DECISIONS.md#shared-school-reference-and-subscription-fallback--4-november-2026).

D-416 to D-419 (5 Nov): the AI helper's three model tiers and provider chain, the free key kept to open tasks, drafts that wait for staff, and cover-period reports; see [AI decisions](ai/DECISIONS.md#ai-helper-best-model-per-task-and-drafts-that-wait-for-staff--5-november-2026).

| Decision | Outcome | Evidence and reason |
|---|---|---|
| D-399 | **Built-in AI has one production switch, `VPPSONE_BUILTIN_AI` (`live` or `test`); unset is off.** A confined deployment is never more than `test` | The pilot refused production outright. The switch lets it go live in two steps (fictional TEST check, then everyone) and off again with one env change. A daily limit of 0 pauses it without a deploy |
| D-400 | **An AI job works in the person's own session, and a test account works only in TEST, in code and in SQL** | `builtinAiSession` uses `resolveSessionLabel` and `canAccessSession`. Migration `20261104055339` drops the TEST-only check on `ai_jobs`, gives `reserve_ai_job` a session, and has cover apply/Undo take the session from the job; each refuses a test account outside TEST. Proved on wtgx with a rolled-back probe |
| D-401 | **Real school records never go to Gemini's free tier** | Google's unpaid terms forbid personal information. Real-session jobs start at Claude, then Gemini Paid; the free key serves TEST material only |
| D-405 | **Bolo understands "X is absent today, arrange her cover": a `teacher_absence` request marks the teacher away (whole day, a half, or spoken periods) and proposes cover for each lesson — a named substitute, otherwise the guided planner's own best free teacher — saved only on Save through the same `arrangeDay` command and plan fingerprint** | Owner, 4 Nov: "maya test mam is absent today unka substitute laga do … didn't complete the task". The only cover action needed period, class and substitute spoken, so the model invented "period 1" and stopped. A single-period `substitution` may now leave out the class (the teacher's one lesson then) and the substitute (the planner's choice). Teachers are matched with honorifics dropped ("mam", "ji", "sir", "मैडम"); a name that fits two people is refused |
| D-406 | **Bolo's listener gets this session's teacher names and class labels as spelling hints, never pupils' names** | The 13:06 recording of "Maya test mam" was transcribed "Mr. Sharma". Names and classes go in the request context (Gemini, Claude) and the OpenAI transcription prompt, for people who arrange cover; a value must still appear in the transcript to be used |
| D-408 | **A free-key daily quota blocks the free key until midnight Pacific in the database, and every job records its key type (free / paid / credits)** | Owner, 4 Nov ("wire the free Gemini key"). Quota, rate, billing, permission and credential errors leave a stage at once. A refused free key is retried after ten minutes, a rate limit after one. D-411 supersedes the original provider order; D-401 still applies |
| D-409 | **Keys are checked on the server (Settings → Built-in AI → Check keys), not from pulled env files** | `vercel env pull` returns placeholders for secret variables (checked: 11-character stand-ins), so production keys never leave Vercel. The check sends one text and one image probe per Gemini model on the free key, the paid key's model, Claude Sonnet and Haiku with structured output, and one OpenAI transcription; results keep fixed failure categories only |
| D-410 | **Bolo survives a model that leaves fields out: Claude gets 18 s and one fixed voice schema; a missing teacher or day is read from the transcript only when beyond doubt** | Live, 4 Nov: Claude Haiku timed out at the 9 s cap (structured-output schemas compile on first use, and Bolo's per-role schema changed per person); Gemini Flash-Lite returned `teacher_absence` without `teacher` or `day` for "Maya test madam will be absent tomorrow…". The one listed teacher named in the transcript, and an unambiguous "today/tomorrow/yesterday/aaj", fill the gap; "kal" never does |
| D-414 | **Claude answers voice commands in a format with no optional fields; an empty value means "not said" and is dropped before the usual check** | Live, 4 Nov 15:19: every Claude voice attempt that day timed out (7 of 7) while Sonnet read cover photos in 4–5 s and a one-field Haiku probe answered in 2.1 s. Anthropic compiles each output schema into a grammar and each optional field roughly doubles it (limit 24 optional fields; compile timeout 180 s); `voiceSpec.validate` has 20, so the first compile outlasted the 18 s stage every time and Gemini Paid answered every voice command. `claudeVoiceOutput` has 0 optional and 0 union fields (`tests/builtin-claude-voice-format.test.ts`); Gemini keeps `voiceSpec`. Details: `docs/ai/DECISIONS.md` |
| D-415 | **`GEMINI_MODEL` is `gemini-3.5-flash-lite` for Production and Preview** | 15:15 key check on production: the free key answered no model (403 billing on four current Flash models, 404 on deprecated 2.5 Flash), so no free model could be ranked. The paid key answered text in 1.5 s and counted 3 of 3 circles on 3.5 Flash-Lite, the cheapest current Flash. Re-run the check once the free key is replaced; change the model only if one answers both probes there |
| D-407 | **Voice-typed words in notes show the moment Chrome hears them: the live words sit in a small store outside React state, and only the boxes that show them re-render** | Owner, 4 Nov: "the note voice typing is not quick, it takes time to write, it must be instant". Each partial result (several a second) re-rendered the 1,200-line composer and new words faded in over 0.3 s. Handlers read the store before `stop()`, so nothing is lost; a test asserts no re-render of the owning screen |
| D-403 | **The phone's "+" sheet opens Quick substitute: Photo (camera on the same tap, then the register is read at once), Speak (Bolo with a cover example) and Arrange.** Shown only to people who may arrange cover (`substitutions:manage`) while built-in AI is on for their session, never while previewing a role; it replaces the plain "Assign substitute" tile | Owner, 4 Nov: "in Plus sign a teacher or admin can quickly assign substitutes by taking photo or speaking". Teachers hold `substitutions:view` only, so a teacher sees it once given a role that may arrange cover; no permission was widened. The photo and Bolo code loads only when tapped (`LazyQuickCover`), so the bar every screen carries grows by the row alone |
| D-404 | **Voice typing in notes rebuilds each listening session from all of Chrome's results and merges restated phrases; `stop()` hands the text over at once and ignores later results** | Android Chrome in continuous mode resends earlier phrases as final, and sends finals after stop; appending each final repeated words, and the late finals reappeared in the next dictation. Listening now continues across Chrome's silence cut-off until Stop (45 s idle limit). A single repeated word is kept ("dheere dheere"); only repeated runs of two or more words are merged. `tests/vx-speech-text.test.tsx` replays the Android pattern |
| D-402 | **Valid cover-photo rows auto-apply in real sessions with the two-minute Undo; WhatsApp to staff stays off until the school turns on staff notices** | The plan asks for auto-apply. Only server-validated, dated, clear rows apply; `VPPSONE_BUILTIN_AI_COVER_AUTO=0` makes it review-only. Notices queue held like every cover notice; `STAFF_NOTICE_TRANSPORT` is unchanged (rule 8 spirit: a real WhatsApp is the school's switch) |
| D-464 | **On a phone the tab bar steps aside for the keyboard, and the page never grows sideways** | Teachers 17 and 19 (6 Nov) found the round centre tab sitting on "Save note". Two causes: the New note screen slides in with `translateX(28px)`, which briefly made the page 400–418 px wide, so Chrome widened the layout viewport and the fixed tab bar ended 50 px above the screen edge; and the bar was lifted by `--keyboard-offset` onto the keyboard, over every save bar. Now `.vx-main` is `overflow-x: clip` below 900 px (clip, not hidden, so sticky bars still stick). The tabs still ride `--keyboard-offset` for browser chrome that covers the layout bottom (receipt navigation), and `--vx-bottom-h` includes that lift so sticky save bars sit on top of them. While a real keyboard is up (`data-kb`, offset over 120 px) the tabs are hidden and `--vx-bottom-h` becomes the keyboard height, so save bars sit on the keyboard |

## Review plan finish and AI connection — 4 November 2026

build a safe, low-cost MCP. The evidence for each requirement ID is in
