Rule 2 is rewritten rather than ignored, and PLAN §9's "native full School Fees module" moves from not-selected to selected |
| D-179 | Fee writes go through the service-role client, and the permission map is the security boundary | Fee RLS keys on `public.users.role`, and 24 of this app's 28 staff profiles have no `public.users` row — so RLS would refuse nearly every fee write. The alternative is creating those rows, which is a write to `public` and a second place a person's role is recorded that can disagree with the first. Janmejay chose the service-role path. It is only safe because it is enforced: `tests/architecture.test.ts` requires every entry point that writes fee data to authorize, by permission or by a machine credential |
| D-180 | `office` maps to the fee app's `view_only`: reads the fee screens, takes no money | A real reduction — office staff post payments in the fee app today — and a deliberate one. Folding the fee app in is the moment to stop granting the power to take money by job title; posting is now granted one person at a time through `accountant`. Neither fee role gets this app's `students:view`, which opens the school directory PLAN §3 reserves for admin and office; the fee desk has its own student screen with its own money on it |
| D-181 | Two message catalogues, one language column, and `both` maps to Hindi on the fee screens | 2,094 next-intl keys on one side and 750 Hindi/English pairs on the other; converting either direction inside a 709-file port is how the port becomes a rewrite. `preferred_language` stays the only stored preference and `feeLocaleFor()` derives the fee locale from it. There is no bilingual fee catalogue and there should not be: it would print 2,094 strings shaped "हिन्दी · English" onto money tables the fee app deliberately designed as single-line. `primary()` already leads with Hindi for a `both` reader, so both halves of the app agree on the lead line. Hinglish has no equivalent here and stays reachable only through the fee app's own switcher and the cookie it writes — which is why the cookie beats the account in `i18n/request.ts` |
| D-182 | Icons are never sent from a Server Component to the nav bars | A `LucideIcon` is a `forwardRef` object; neither it nor an element built from one survives the RSC boundary in a **production** build, and both were tried before this was the answer. Each failed only in prod and worked in `next dev`, which is what made it expensive. `navIconFor(href)` lives in `platform/config/navigation.ts`, which is pure, so the bars import it as an ordinary client import and there is no boundary to cross. The fee app's own note about this — about its command palette — had been read and acted on two props away from where it mattered |
| D-183 | "Ported" is defined once, in the codemod, and read by the tests and ESLint | `scripts/port-rewrite-imports.mjs` has to know which trees came from School Fees because that is what it rewrites; `tests/architecture.test.ts` and `eslint.config.mjs` import `PORTED_TREES` from it rather than keeping a second copy. Two lists agree today and drift the day somebody adds a module — silently, and in the direction that does not fail: a new module would simply be exempt from the rule standing between it and the school's money |
| D-184 | Every button is at least 44px tall on a phone, including the fee app's `sm` | The fee app ships `sm` at 40px. PLAN §3a holds a stricter line — 44px minimum, 46–48 for principal actions — because these screens are used standing up with a register in the other hand, and `responsive-baseline.spec.ts` asserts it. A deliberate divergence from the fee app that costs nobody anything: the suite caught six screens where a 40px button had arrived, including "Take attendance", which is the one control a teacher taps every morning |
| D-185 | The fee area names a missing fee configuration once, at its layout, rather than crashing | `schoolfees-dev` carries the fee schema (67 tables, every RPC, all three matviews) and none of the fee rows — `fee_policy_configs`, `app_settings`, `installments`, `payments` and `receipts` are empty. So `getActiveSessionLabel()` honestly returns `""` and the first screen to validate it throws. A crash is the wrong answer to "this has not been set up": it reads as a broken application rather than an unconfigured one, which is the same mistake as rendering ₹0 for a figure that is merely unavailable (D-172) |
| D-186 | `schoolfees-dev` now carries a de-identified replica of the live fee database, and the money in it is exact | The fee screens had never been opened against fee data, so nothing about them had been tested; and inventing a fee policy puts fabricated money on a screen somebody might believe (F6). `scripts/replicate-live.mjs` copies 11,295 rows across 34 tables from the live project, read-only and structurally so — the live client is sealed inside a closure whose only exported function calls `.select()`, so no code path in the file can write to live. Every amount, due date, receipt number and instalment plan is carried across unchanged: dev totals ₹1,56,19,700 billed and ₹57,30,757 receipted, to the rupee, the same as live. Every name, phone number, address and free-text note is replaced; Aadhaar, Jan-Aadhaar, APAAR and photographs are dropped rather than masked, because a government identifier has no test value worth carrying a copy of. It is recognisable as test data by construction: twelve surnames across 614 children, every phone in the unroutable 99999 block, every address ending `.invalid`, and a row in `vppsone.data_replica` recording the run |
| D-187 | The academic session is read from `app_settings.active_session_label`, never inferred by sorting the labels | Six places derived "the session the school is in" by sorting every `session_label` and taking the last — `resolveSessionLabel()` (92 call sites), the nightly job runner, three seeding scripts and the e2e harness. All six were correct on a database with one session and wrong on any database with more: **`TEST-2026-27` sorts above both `2027-28` and `2026-27`**, because `T` sorts above `2`, and the live fee database has carried that rehearsal session for months. So on live every register, mark, cover plan and dashboard would have worked in the rehearsal year — reading empty, writing there, raising nothing. `pickSessionLabel()` in `platform/config/active-session.ts` holds the order of preference once, with no database in it, and `tests/active-session.test.ts` asserts the `TEST-` case directly. It could not be seen until dev held more than one session, which is what D-186 produced |
| D-188 | Sixteen of the eighteen fee RPCs are gated on `public.users.role`, and ten of those cannot be reached from this app at all | `has_permission()` is `auth.uid() is not null and case private.current_staff_role() …`, and `current_staff_role()` reads only `public.users`. So a VPPS One account fails it through the cookie client (no row, by D-6) **and** through the service-role client (`auth.uid()` is null). Six RPCs carry a `coalesce(auth.role(),'') = 'service_role'` arm and work once called with the service-role client — that is D-179's pattern and `preview_workbook_payment_allocation` is fixed that way here. Ten do not, and the list includes `post_student_payment_with_adjustments`: **an accountant in VPPS One cannot post a payment**, and no client choice changes that. This is a product decision, not a bug to patch quietly — the options are a single shared fee service identity in `public.users`, a fallback in `current_staff_role()` to `vppsone.staff_profiles`, or per-staff rows (already rejected). It is recorded rather than chosen |
| D-189 | Dev is repaired to match live where they drift, and the repair is not a migration | All three financial materialized views had no grants at all in `schoolfees-dev`, where live grants them to `authenticated` and `service_role` — so the Payment Desk answered 503 "permission denied for materialized view" for every account, with or without data. The grants were applied to dev directly and deliberately **not** written as a migration: a migration is something that can be replayed against live, live already has them, and rule 8 makes a write to live a stop-and-ask. The earlier claim that dev is a schema twin was checked on columns and not on privileges; the two are twins on 68 tables' columns and were not on grants |
| D-190 | A fixture states its own preconditions, and the session it seeds into is the one the app reads | `qa:seed` printed "run npm run qa:seed" as the remedy for a precondition it never created: `fan-out.spec.ts` needs three classes with one class teacher each, two reachable by phone and one not, and the fixture made two class teachers and set no phones. The third had been set by hand once and was invisible until a rebuild removed it, at which point a correct test failed and looked broken. The fixture now creates all three and sets reachability in both directions — explicitly absent as well as explicitly present, because a profile that merely happens to have no number is a fixture that breaks the day somebody fills one in |
| D-191 | `private.current_staff_role()` falls back to `vppsone.staff_profiles`, and that is how VPPS One takes money | D-188 recorded that an accountant could not post a payment and left the choice open; Janmejay's answer was to fix it. Three options were on the table and this is the one that keeps the person: signing the fee RPCs as one shared service identity in `public.users` also works, but then `auth.uid()` inside the function is the machine, so `created_by` on every receipt records the machine rather than the accountant who took the money — too high a price on an append-only receipts table. **The fee database was already prepared for this**: `public.staff_role` carries all eight VPPS One roles in dev *and* in live, and `public.has_permission()` already has a case arm for each; only this function had never been told to look anywhere but `public.users`. Purely additive — every fee-app user has a row there, so the first branch still answers for all of them and no existing behaviour changes. It unblocks all sixteen gated RPCs at once, and the boundaries hold at the database: accountant posts and may not undo, admin undoes, office and fee_collector and teacher are refused, verified by asking each of them. **It is a change to a function School Fees owns and must be applied to the live fee database at cutover** — see the migration header |
| D-192 | Anything a cached function reads goes through `getCacheSafeClient()`, never the cookie client | The Payment Desk rendered "The protected workspace hit an error" — `cookies()` inside `unstable_cache()`, from `getReceiptReversalTotals`. What made it invisible is the early return above it: with no receipt ids there is nothing to look up, so the cookie client was never created. "No receipt ids" is exactly what an empty day produces. **So the desk worked every morning and broke on the first payment of the day, every day**, and `schoolfees-dev` had no receipts at all until a replica put some there. The same shape hid a second one: `getRepaymentPlanCoverageForStudents` returns early when there are no active plans, and the `.in()` bug above it guaranteed there were none — fixing that bug is what made its cookie client start being created. Two silent failures, each concealing the next |
| D-193 | An over-long PostgREST URL is refused at once, by the client, with a message that names the fix | `.in(...)` serialises into the request URL; past ~300 uuids `fetch` stalls eight seconds and rejects with a bare `TypeError: fetch failed`, which `const { data } = await …` turns into an empty array. Chunking each call site is the real fix and there are over 150 of them, so `createGuardedFetch` is the net underneath: one length comparison per request, no stall, and an error naming the table and pointing at `fetchInChunks`. It cannot make a discarded error loud — nothing at that layer can — but it turns a bug somebody lives with into one somebody finds. It delegates to `globalThis.fetch` at call time rather than capturing a reference: Next replaces the global `fetch` with an instrumented one, and a captured reference hands `unstable_cache` a function from the wrong scope |
| D-194 | Mobile symmetry preserves meaning and does not depend on clipping | The 30 September refresh centers the workspace and gives cards/buttons equal gutters and icon/check slots, while leaving names/reasons left-aligned and wrapping. Enlarged/Hindi tab labels may grow the navigation, so the scroll clearance follows the rendered border-box height rather than a fixed 68px assumption. A sheet is named and owns modal focus/inert/scroll state; More carries the native role's destinations, not a menu guessed from the fee-role translation |
| D-195 | Optional financial chrome and background warming have explicit workload bounds | Native roles without `fees:view` do not start fee-summary/policy reads for shell chrome. Background prefetch skips constrained/offline/hidden states and duplicate 60-second warming. These are query/request-budget improvements, not claims of measured Core Web Vitals. New animations use opacity/transform only and are silenced under reduced motion; the workspace itself only fades to preserve fixed payment CTAs |
| D-196 | Small metadata text must keep full readable strength on paper and ink | Authenticated local smoke reproduced 3.19–3.36:1 small gray text plus weakened bilingual/accent copy on native teacher/admin/office/viewer homes. VPPS overrides the subtle foreground to 42% lightness on paper and 68% on ink, keeps the shared token names and print reset legible, and removes alpha reductions from essential native copy. Thirteen token-contrast guards and native rendered-content regressions pin this deliberate accessibility divergence from the fee reference; it does not change financial policy or data |
| D-197 | A timetable class-label read checks the capability of its consumer | Teacher and view-only staff have `timetable:view` but not `academics:view`; sharing the academics loader made an authorized timetable throw. A separate `listTimetableClasses` entry point checks `timetable:view` before the same private SELECT. Existing academics consumers retain their gate; roles and policies are unchanged |
| D-198 | Directory scope filters fit bounded URLs and pagination merges globally | The session directory sent ~511 UUIDs in one rejected 19,925-character URL. Cookie/RLS enrolments now page in stable 200-row ranges; disjoint 100-ID SQL searches return only matching IDs/names. The server counts and alphabetically merges the indexes with fixed English collation and an ID tie-break, clamps/slices the global page, then loads contact fields for at most 50 IDs. No per-chunk pagination, out-of-scope student reads, browser roster transfer, or database changes |
| D-199 | Native admin overview has its own responsive composition | PR 37 changed shared navigation but missed native School overview. Keep Panel and non-admin homes stable; use a server-rendered admin component with existing facts, counts, owners and destinations. Put support before workspaces in phone DOM order and beside attention on desktop. Actual TEST screenshots and user approval now gate release. |
| D-200 | A dashboard distinguishes unfinished work, known absence-derived facts and unavailable reads | Pending and unread attention entries lead; zero entries remain smaller links with their owners/counts. Only real fractions draw tracks. Teaching staff not marked away is active teaching roll minus recorded absences, not confirmed attendance. Carry the existing cover read failure separately from no published plan. External live workspaces announce their data boundary and action; secondary permitted screens remain in a native keyboard-operable disclosure. The user's 30 September approval of the actual design and these five refinements authorizes release after verification. |
| D-201 | The installed app is the whole role-aware VPPS One workspace, with a neutral `/` start | Windows Chrome/Edge and Android use the browser's installation flow; iPhone Safari gets Add to Home Screen guidance in Preferences. The existing saved start screen and permission guards choose the destination. The worker caches public assets and a neutral reconnect page only; protected HTML, private JSON, collection tokens and mutations stay online. An update waits for an explicit reload after draft writes settle and refuses while a form is busy or storage failed. |
| D-202 | Already-loaded attendance, marks and collection forms keep account/session/record-scoped drafts, with manual recovery | IndexedDB is primary with a browser-storage fallback, serialized writes, confirmed status and deletion tombstones. A recovered or changed-server draft is reviewed before submission. Public collection keys use a token digest. Financial drafts use the same scoped store, while posting stays online with the existing permission and `client_request_id` contract. Old unowned drafts are not replayed. These are device drafts, not proof of a school save or a promise of offline cold access. |
| D-203 | Performance changes have measured payload evidence and a route chunk budget, with sampled coarse telemetry | The root ships only shell translations; fee pages load their catalogue locally. Non-finance roles skip finance shell reads, commands load on demand, prefetch is bounded and academic session reads deduplicate per request. Teacher/admin homes put overview first and desktop student selection is local. The directory queries a bounded ID index and only one page of contact details, using a fresh mutable PostgREST builder per chunk. CI rejects over 10 percent gzip chunk growth per measured route; this is a regression proxy, not browser transfer or a field speed guarantee. Authenticated performance batches retain only fixed event names, coarse routes, role, viewport bucket and installed state. |
| D-204 | A payment draft can write only after its exact owner/session/student/date key has loaded | Reproduced student draft deletion and date draft overwrite on the pre-fix desk using synthetic device storage. Readiness belongs to the current key; cancelled loads/writes/discards cannot update another selection, failed reads do not authorize writes, and owner/session changes clear the previous in-memory amount. Discard clears a carried amount without re-saving it and respects newer edits. Payment drafts contain no school-record baseline, so recovery uses the neutral review message. The 1 October user approved fixes and tests only: this focused branch opens a draft PR and remains unmerged and undeployed pending explicit release approval. |
| D-205 | Role, activation and login linking are the administrator's alone; the office keeps the records | Raj, 4 October, before P1 began. The office held `staff:manage` and `updateStaffAction` accepted any role, so it could promote a linked colleague to administrator or switch one off — and through `feeRoleFor` an administrator is a fee administrator with password resets. Now one database command, `vppsone.change_staff_authority`, is the only writer of `role` and `is_active`: admin-only, re-reads the actor at call time, refuses self-changes, appends `staff_authority_changes`, and a trigger keeps at least one active administrator under an advisory lock. The office still edits names, numbers and the teaching flag, and may create teacher and view-only profiles (`rolesAssignableBy`). No app code writes `user_id`; linking stays an out-of-band admin act |
| D-206 | Issuing a report card needs `assessments:manage` | It wrote a frozen `report_cards` row under `reports:view`, which the office and both coordinators hold. Reading a report and issuing one are different acts; the exam coordinator and the administrator issue. P8 replaces issuing with immutable revisions; this closes the door until then |
| D-207 | The fee database's role precedence is left as D-191 made it, and the decision it needs is named | `private.current_staff_role()` prefers an active `public.users` row and falls through an inactive one to `vppsone.staff_profiles`. So a person switched off in `/staff` who is still active in `public.users` keeps fee-RPC authority from a direct JWT (they cannot sign in to VPPS One: admission reads only the profile), and an office member who is an accountant in `public.users` can post through the RPC. Both are the fee app's existing authority, not new exposure, and changing a shared fee function is outside this session's authorization. **Before cutover:** decide whether the profile becomes authoritative in that function (and an inactive public row denies), and whether `/fees/staff` keeps writing `public.users` at all |
| D-208 | Every server-side Supabase client checks its target before it is made, and an unfamiliar target is refused | P0. `assertPermittedTarget()` reads the project ref from the URL's hostname exactly. Dev reaches the dev project or a local Supabase; the live fee project only from `VPPSONE_ENV=live` and never under a test runner; unknown refs fail closed; CI's placeholder host only on CI. The browser clients are not guarded because `VPPSONE_ENV` never reaches them and they would refuse the cutover; no page reaches the browser without a guarded server render. Scripts share `scripts/target-guard.mjs`, replacing eight `url.includes(ref)` checks |
| D-209 | Direct reads follow the app's own scope: reference data for active staff, pupil data by permission and class, a cover duty grants nothing | P1. Every `using (true)` policy is replaced. `vppsone.my_class_keys()` is class teacher over the whole effective interval plus the published timetable — the same rule `classScopeFor()` applies in the pages, which now refuse another class rather than render it empty. Covering a period is a reason to be in the room, not to read the class's history. Phones, absence reasons and link tokens are column-revoked and read by the app through the service role after the permission that shows them. `npm run verify:access` signs in as all thirteen kinds of account and proves it against PostgREST |
| D-210 | Cover is planned against physical lessons rebuilt from the slots, and grouping is forced only by one person in several classes | P2. A joint lesson (one teacher, several sections, one period) is one event and one need, and its substitute must fit every section — ranked against each, worst tier wins. A split class is two cohorts. A team block is never arranged automatically until the school states how many of the team must be present (`team_supervision_rules`); with no rule, any absence from a team is a visible needs-check. Two teachers teaching the same subject at the same time are always two lessons. Events are derived on read, deterministically keyed, and not stored: the slots stay the single source |
| D-211 | Bells are school data, versioned, and a begun period is never arranged | Raj, 4 October: the Timetable2025 regular bells as the editable default. `bell_schedules` holds one current row per session; every edit bumps `revision`, which the cover command names and checks. Time is school wall-clock in Asia/Kolkata, computed from a fixed +05:30 offset (no DST). A period the bells do not describe is "unknown" and treated as not arrangeable |
| D-212 | Every cover change is one database command over the whole day's desired state, and the browser sends only the intent | P3. Save absence, back at school, assign, release and arrange again all call `commit_cover_command`: serialised per day, idempotent per submission id, refused when the day, timetable or bells moved, re-validated against the facts at commit, and atomic across absence, duties, notice intents and receipt. The server recomputes the plan from the intent; nothing the browser says about eligibility or ownership is trusted. There is no draft and no publish: what the coordinator sees is what teachers see. The old plan tables stay as read-only history |
| D-213 | What cannot be covered safely is "needs help", never self study or a merged class by default | P3. The old planner turned every open period into a merged class or self study automatically, with no capacity, room or supervision check. Now an unarrangeable lesson is a visible needs-help duty with its reason and, where the engine has one, a suggestion a person may accept. Only teaching staff are automatic candidates (an active office profile is not classroom eligibility); a person may choose anybody active, and a hard block still refuses them |
| D-214 | A staff notice is accepted only with a provider receipt; anything uncertain is Unknown and only a person resends it | P4. Claim under a lease after re-checking the duty, stamp what will be sent, then finish. Timeout, crash after the call and a 2xx without an id are Unknown, never retried by the machine; definite errors and rate limits retry with bounded backoff while the notice is still useful. Transport is `STAFF_NOTICE_TRANSPORT`: dev defaults to the mock, live to disabled, `aisensy` needs a key and the provider gate. Operator switches stop new commands or dispatch without deleting anything |
| D-215 | A duty link is a pointer, never a permission, and the teacher's answers are not facts about the classroom | P5. `/duty/[id]` authenticates, then the row decides: holder, former holder, cover staff, or nobody. Acknowledge means seen; "I cannot cover" escalates to the academic coordinators (administrators if none) and leaves the duty with her until a command reassigns it, after which she is never asked for it again. GET changes nothing |
| D-216 | Choosing, by hand, the teacher who already holds a duty pins it in place, with no new revision and no notice | P3, found writing the browser journey. The diff in `commit_cover_command` compared only what a teacher was told — status, holder, reason, classes, who is away — so pressing Assign on the planner's own choice was "unchanged": the screen said Saved, `source` stayed `auto`, `pinned` stayed false, and the next re-arrangement was free to move her, which is what the button exists to prevent. A new revision would have been the wrong fix: it sends her a "changed" notice about nothing. So the unchanged branch now carries `source` and `pinned` forward in place, like suggestions and warnings, and the receipt counts it (`pinned`) so the screen says "confirmed and pinned". Migration `*_assign_same_teacher_pins`; `verify:cover` check 15 |
| D-217 | A register is recorded only by a save of the whole roll for its date, and the roll is the enrolment record's for that date | P6, plan §12. Before, a register could be submitted with children simply missing, and every reader had to interpret the gaps; "present" also arrived by a Mark-all button that recorded nothing anyone checked. Now opening records nothing, every child shows present only as a preview, and `save_attendance_register` refuses a save that does not name every child on that date's roll exactly once — naming who joined or left. The roll is `vppsone.attendance_roster()`: enrolled on or before the date, and still on the roll that day, with the leaving date counted as the last day. One rule, in SQL, read by the page and by the save |
| D-218 | The daily register is the class teacher's or assistant's as of today, or the office's on their behalf; a subject teacher asks for a correction | Plan: "daily attendance belongs to the class teacher or explicit delegate". Before, anyone with a teaching assignment in the class could take it. A former class teacher keeps nothing — ownership is read as of today, the same rule `my_class_keys()` uses. The office marks on behalf (recorded) and corrects any register; the class teacher corrects her own directly, with a reason; anybody else in scope asks, and approving the request is the same command, so the correction and the approval commit together. The database applies all of it again inside the save |
| D-219 | A correction is a new revision with a reason; nothing about a recorded register is edited in place | `attendance_register_revisions` keeps every saved state — who, when, why, the whole register and the before-and-after of what changed — append-only and scoped exactly like its register. The register itself carries its revision, so a save against an older one is refused as stale with the receipt it lost to. The 11 registers already submitted on dev became revision 1, kind `imported`; a register an older build submits during the deploy window is imported the same way the first time anybody saves it |
| D-220 | A half day counts as the school's credit, 0.5 by default, set once per session for every percentage | `attendance_rules.half_day_credit`, edited on /academics by `academics:manage`. The register, the student card, the monthly sheet and the report card all read `attendancePercent(statuses, rules)`; there is no second copy of the rule. Late stays a whole day and leave stays out of both halves, as before |
| D-221 | The attendance reminder is a staff notice, after the first period, to whoever answers for the register that day | It was a drafted message at 11:00 that nothing could take back. Now the job runs at 04:00 UTC (09:30 in school; a daily cron fires late, never early), asks nobody before the first period has ended by the bells, and queues `attendance_reminder` notices in the P4 outbox keyed by class, day and person — so a re-run adds nothing and running it by hand at noon reminds only who is still outstanding. The outbox re-checks at the moment it sends: a register saved since, or a teacher no longer responsible, is suppressed. Live stays `disabled` until the provider gate, like cover notices |
| D-222 | Attendance waiting on a phone is never replayed without a person | The plan forbids silently replaying attendance offline. The old form flushed a queued register when the connection came back; the queue module beside it was never imported. Both are gone: marks are kept on the device as a draft scoped to account, session, class and day, restored only when the person chooses, and sent only when they press Save — after which a lost answer is asked about with the same submission id, never resent blind |
| D-223 | Exempt is a fourth state of a mark, out of both halves of a total | P7, plan §13: blank, zero, absent and exempt/not applicable stay distinct. Before, an excused child could only be left blank — and the paper never finished — or marked absent, which is a nought on a report. `marks.exempt` is its own column, and a constraint keeps it from carrying a value or an absence. `totalsFor` leaves it out of both halves, as leave is in the register; `passed` returns null; the report card, the CSV and the change log each name it |
| D-224 | A paper's marks are saved by one command — draft, submit, correction, reopen — and submitting completes the teacher's task without approving anything | Before, a column saved cell by cell, so a failure half-way left half a column written, and nothing said a teacher had finished. `save_marks` checks the actor, the exam, the revision the page showed, every cell against the paper's scheme and the exam's roll, and for a submission that nobody is blank. It then writes the changed cells, their log, the new revision and a receipt in one transaction; any refusal leaves the column untouched. After submitting, a change is a correction with a reason, and never back to blank; the exam office alone reopens. The paper's scheme (maximum, step, exemption) cannot change once marks are being entered |
| D-225 | Marks belong to the subject teacher the timetable assigns, or to the exam office | Plan: subject teachers enter their own marks; open only the assigned class and subject. Before, a teacher with any link to a class — class teacher or any subject — could type every subject's marks there. Now entry needs a `teaching_assignments` row for that class and subject, or `assessments:manage`; a class teacher reads her class's other papers read-only. The missing-marks nudge asks the subject teacher too |
| D-226 | A report card is worked out from an approved, per-term scheme it keeps a copy of | P8, plan §13. The grade bands were a constant nobody at the school had confirmed, and a change to them would have moved every card. `report_schemes` holds the bands, the rounding place, an optional pass mark, the attendance period, the header and the signature lines; an approved revision never changes (a trigger refuses it) and a change is the next revision. The percentage is the unrounded fraction rounded once, half up on the decimal value, and graded on that number — so 32.95% at one place is 33.0 and in a band starting at 33. Until the school approves its own, the default is the CBSE-style set, and every screen that uses it says so |
| D-227 | An issued card is append-only: a reissue is a new revision that says why and keeps the old | Issuing upserted one row per child and term, so a reissue silently replaced what a family held. `report_card_issues` keeps every revision with its card, scheme, sources (each paper's entry revision, the attendance period and what was not recorded), who, when and — for a reissue — why; the old revision is superseded and linked, never edited or deleted. The one exception, in the trigger and said there, is a synthetic `QA-…` term the tests create and remove. `report_cards` is kept, no longer written; its one dev row was imported as revision 1 |
| D-228 | A card is released only when every paper the class sat has been submitted, nothing is missing, and no mark has moved since it was worked out | The plan's "block incomplete release". `issue_report_card` checks, inside the transaction that would write the card: the actor runs the exams; the scheme is the term's latest approved one; every paper the child's class sat that term is submitted (P7); the card has nothing missing; and each paper's entry revision is the one the card was worked out from — a mark corrected between preview and release is refused, naming the subject, rather than issued from stale numbers |
| D-229 | Strings for a screen a handful of staff open live in a second catalogue, `src/messages/setup.ts` (`ms`), so they ship only with that screen | P8's PR failed the route bundle budget: `/students` grew past 10% over its baseline (135.8 KB against 135.2) without a line of its code changing. `src/messages/index.ts` is imported by the shell, so every pair in it is in every page's JavaScript, and each slice's scheme, history and rule strings were being paid for by the attendance register and the student list. The rule is unchanged — every string a pair, no hard-coded Hindi, the same language test over both catalogues and no key in both — and the catalogue-mixing rule counts `@/messages/setup` as this app's. Moved: the report scheme and history, and the half-day rule (39 pairs, `/students` 134.7 KB). New set-up and policy screens put their strings there from the start; anything the shell, the register or a home screen says stays in `m` |
| D-230 | A parent message is approved against a snapshot of what it rests on, and the approval is checked again when the send is recorded | P9, plan §14. D-10 kept the app from handing a parent message to a provider, but a message queued on Monday could go on Thursday to a number the family had changed, about a request since corrected, with nothing to say so. Now queueing a parent message pins its destination, the guardian number on the child's record, the version of what it is about, a hash of its text and its category's policy version. "Approve and open WhatsApp" compares that with today's and refuses, naming every difference, before giving the link; "I sent it" compares again. The policy is per category and needs a person's approval by default. An automatic exception exists only when it names recipients, trigger, content and correction (a database constraint, so a blank does not count), and the app dispatches to a parent only with the school's `parent_dispatch` switch on, a named exception and nothing stale. On dev, and at release, every category needs approval and the switch is off |
| D-231 | A send that happened is recorded whatever changed, and what changed stays on the row as a correction owed | The plan: never coalesce away a real send. If the record moved between approving and pressing send, the send is still recorded — it is a disclosure that happened — and the reasons are written on the row before it becomes `sent`, because a sent row is never rewritten afterwards. The screen reads them from the row, so they survive a reload and do not depend on the browser that pressed the button |
| D-232 | A wrong number is a kept flag that stops its use until an administrator clears it with a note | Anyone who works the messages can report a parent number as wrong, saying why. Every unsent message to it is withdrawn with that reason (a sent one is untouched), the number is listed at the top of the messages screen, and a new parent message to it is refused at queueing, not caught later. Clearing is an administrator's, with a note, and the flag is kept. Office staff read flags; teachers do not (`collection:view`) |
| D-233 | A child's collected values have one writer, and every write is kept | P10, plan §11 ("accepted native overlay with field provenance") and "one writer per entity". Accepting was two statements from the app: mark the submissions, then upsert `student_records` for every row it had *read*. Two people deciding at once could both write, and the value a record held before was gone. Now `vppsone.write_student_value()` is the only writer. It applies precedence and appends to `student_record_history` (append-only; the one exception is a synthetic `qa_` field the tests remove, as with D-227). The app reaches it only through `accept_submissions`, one transaction that re-checks `collection:manage` and decides only what is still pending. It is idempotent per command id, and the ids travel in the body, so four hundred confirmations are one call. An architecture test refuses any `.from("student_records")` write in `src/`. The one record already held got a `held_before_history` row, so every value has a provenance |
| D-234 | Who wins: a person over an import, an import only over itself, and an older answer never over a newer decision without a person choosing it | Ported from SamPark's `precedence.ts` (S29), in SQL rather than in the caller. An import never replaces a value a teacher or the office recorded, and may correct only what the same import (`source_detail`) wrote; anything else it offers is kept in the history as `blocked`, with the reason, rather than dropped. A person replaces anything — but `accept_submissions` refuses, by name, an answer with a newer one waiting for the same child and field (`superseded`), and an answer about a value that moved after the teacher saw it (`record_moved`). The second is accepted only with `replace_newer`, which the review screen offers as "Accept and replace the newer value" on exactly those rows; a superseded answer is offered only Reject. The same value confirmed by a person over an import's becomes a person's |
| D-235 | SamPark's records come in from its own Students workbook, matched by ids alone, and what ids cannot settle waits for a person | P10b, plan §11 identity reconciliation. The office exports SamPark's Students workbook and uploads it at `/collection/import`; nothing reads SamPark's database. Each row resolves as a multimap: an earlier mapping (`external_student_ids`, app `sampark`), the SR number against the fee record's `admission_no`, or failing an SR, SamPark's admission number. Leading zeros are kept, and a name or phone never decides. Two ids that point at different children, a number two children share, and two rows for one child are all **ambiguous**; no id at all is **unmatched**. Staging writes nothing to a child's record, and the same file again resumes its batch. `apply_sampark_import` applies matched rows as one replayable command: it records the mapping, which is what lets SamPark's own links name the child, and offers each value to `write_student_value` as an import from SamPark (D-234). A mapping that disagrees with an earlier one in either direction goes back to the queue with both children named. `resolve_sampark_row` is the queue: a person picks a candidate or a child by SR number (`matched_on = hand`), or sets the row aside with a reason; a decided row is not decided again. Only five SamPark columns are read (Mobile No → guardian phone, Date of Birth, Aadhaar Last 4, Address, Bus Route), and every other column is named as not read |
| D-236 | A child's photograph is placed by the office, never matched by name, and a replaced one is kept | P10c, SamPark parity S-11. `/collection/photos` takes a class at a time, the children without a photograph first. A photograph comes from the camera, one file, or a whole folder. Folder placement is ported from SamPark's `photo-filename.ts` (id tokens, an id a filename split in two, two files for one child refused) **without its third tier**, the child's name inside a class, because plan §11 forbids joining pupils by name and a wrong match puts one child's face on another's record. A file whose name holds no id waits in a tray for a person to choose. Photographs are shrunk on the device to 800 px before they travel. They are kept in the private `vppsone-photos` bucket and served only by short-lived signed URLs after a permission check. `place_student_photo` is one replayable command: one current photograph per child; a retake keeps the earlier row and its bytes, pointing at its replacement; and a placement that did not see the current photograph is refused as `stale` instead of being put on top. The fee app's `photo_path` is neither read nor written |
| D-237 | Student and round exports are server-written `.xlsx` workbooks, every cell text, with the counts written inside | P10d, SamPark parity S-13. This reverses SOURCES' note that SamPark's `excel.ts` was not ported (Phase 6.5 kept academic exports as CSV built in the browser). S-13 asks for multi-sheet workbooks for students and rounds, Hindi intact, blank vs zero vs "Absent" preserved, leading-zero SR numbers preserved and row counts that reconcile with the screen. The `xlsx` library already ships server-side with the fee module, so nothing new is installed and nothing reaches the browser bundle. Every cell is written as text with the `@` format, so "0101" stays "0101" even after somebody edits and saves. An About sheet states each sheet's row count. Sheet names are cleaned as SamPark's `safeSheetName`/`uniqueSheetName` clean them. `/collection/export/students` (one row per enrolled child, each collected value, a Sources sheet with provenance) and `/collection/rounds/[id]/export` (a sheet per link: shown, answer, status) are office-only and logged in `academic_exports`. Photographs are not embedded |
| D-238 | An archive is a copy of every VPPS One table and every document and photograph under one manifest, and it is not trusted until a restore drill passes | P11, plan §16 ("accessible preservation"; "test a synthetic restoration of both records and documents before treating a backup as usable"; Supabase backups exclude Storage objects). `/archives` (administrator) builds into the private `vppsone-archives` bucket: each `vppsone` table as the canonical JSON text Postgres wrote, together with its row count and md5 from the same statement and snapshot, and each document and photograph byte with its sha256, plus `manifest.json` (schema version, app commit, totals). Rows travel as text and are never parsed in the app, so a numeric 0.50 cannot come back as 0.5. `restore_drill_table` reloads each file into a scratch table of the same shape inside one transaction and must reproduce the archived digest; every object is re-hashed and one is round-tripped through a scratch prefix; every drill is kept. Nothing is deleted to make an archive, and the fee app's `public` tables are not in it. The one engine (`archive-engine.ts`) is shared by the screen and `verify:archive`. Off-site copies are manual until the school authorizes an automated destination (`docs/ARCHIVE.md`) |
| D-239 | Fee parity is proved from the source boundary and the catalogue, never by posting money | P12 / Prompt 8: "protect fee parity with source-boundary assertions … do not call live fees to prove noninterference; no migration modifies shared fee schemas/functions without separately approved scope". Two sides. An architecture test refuses any migration here that replaces, alters or drops a fee function or trigger, or inserts, updates or deletes `public` rows; its one approved exception is D-191's `current_staff_role` fallback, and the test fails if that file disappears and leaves the allowlist stale. `npm run verify:fee-parity`, read-only through `vppsone.fee_contract_fingerprints()`, asserts the guards the plan names: request-key idempotent receipts, the serialising lock on posting, append-only receipts and payments, protected money columns, a locked, capped, idempotent waiver keeping `manual_collected` provenance, and locked reversals. It also fingerprints all 505 fee functions, triggers and indexes against `docs/evidence/fee-contract-baseline.json`. A synthetic payment was not posted to prove the rollback leaves postings intact: the dev ledger is the de-identified replica, and adding receipts to it needs its own approval. That remains a named, unrun check in the release report |


| D-240 | Opening a manual WhatsApp link does not mark a send; the person confirms after sending | The existing handoff could mark SENT before a popup opened or before a message was sent. Preparation now returns a link with no write; I sent it records the manual confirmation after fresh guards/flags/status checks. Blocked popups get an explicit link, Keep in To send writes nothing, and Undo matches the exact timestamp and manual-only provider null. No automated dispatch is introduced. |
| D-241 | Unreadable authorization and incomplete financial reads refuse a confident answer | Matrix read failure cannot fall back to a role seed that may restore a revoked grant. Receipt close history is session-scoped and fully paged, failed reads are unavailable, incomplete receipt lists cannot close a day. These checks do not serialize concurrent closes; that remains an explicitly open database transition. |
| D-242 | Partial multi-step outcomes are shown honestly and are not called atomic | Inbox and preparation responses can be lost after a side effect. Failure copy asks for reload and queued-message metadata failures acknowledge that messages may already exist. Stable queue IDs and affected-row checks are preserved. Atomic queue/decision, day close, mixed seat set/clear and uncertain provider reconciliation still require separate DB work and authorized integration evidence. |
| D-243 | Previous receipt dates follow paid-session relationships | Dev schema metadata confirmed `receipts.session_label` does not exist. Use the established payment/installment/class inner-join relationship instead, preserve error propagation, and reject invalid receipt columns in the regression mock. No migration or personal record access is needed. |

### Approved student profile (12 October 2026)

The portrait is 96px with initials on missing, denied or failed photographs. Only the private `vppsone.student_photos` source is read, after the unchanged `collection:manage` permission and profile class authorization; it is never combined with `public.students.photo_path`. Overview, Learning, Fees and More group the existing allowed tabs while preserving their canonical URLs and replace-state history. Overview uses recorded attendance, provisional-aware results, recorded strengths and explicit support follow-ups. Fee buckets and concession amounts use the existing adapters unchanged. Scoped readers require a current-session enrollment visible to them; a legacy public class cannot authorize a moved pupil. Failed reads remain unavailable rather than empty. No school database writes, migrations or messages are part of this UI change. The user's later release instruction authorizes the established GitHub PR/green CI/main/Vercel process.

### Payment parity and interrupted attempts (11 October 2026)

The active VX payment entry restores the original Fees controls and proven shortcut/receipt components. Review remains a genuine confirmation step despite the reference fast-Cash shortcut. Selected-date dues come from the existing RPC. Before sending a payment, its exact financial request and form are saved in the existing account/session-scoped draft store; unresolved attempts survive selection/reload and freeze until checked, with retries using the original request id. Recovery storage failure prevents sending. A permanent waiver that succeeded before a rejected payment keeps the same key on retry. Financial refresh is awaited before receipt readback, and WhatsApp remains an explicit action. No migration.


## Actions runner setup - 27 October 2026

Keep every phone matrix check separate and benchmark the matching official Playwright image before release. Use setup-node 24; fail on Playwright version drift. Limit new cancellation to obsolete PR test runs. Do not infer monetary savings from runner durations.
